💡 Core Concepts
📚 What is RAG? (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) Beginner
Understand the fundamentals of RAG — why it exists, how it works, and when to use it.
beginner rag
▼
💡
The Core Problem RAG SolvesLLMs have a static knowledge cutoff. They hallucinate when they don't know something. RAG grounds the LLM in your own, up-to-date data.
🔄 RAG in 3 Steps
1
Retrieve
User query → embed query → vector similarity search → fetch top-K relevant document chunks.
2
Augment
Inject retrieved chunks as context into the LLM prompt alongside the user question.
3
Generate
LLM generates an answer grounded in the retrieved context — cites real sources, not hallucinations.
📊 RAG vs. Fine-tuning vs. Prompt Engineering
ApproachWhen to UseCostFreshness
Prompt EngineeringStatic, small context fits in windowLowStatic
RAGLarge/dynamic knowledge baseMediumReal-time
Fine-tuningSpecific style/format, stable knowledgeHighStatic
RAG + Fine-tuneDomain expertise + dynamic dataVery HighReal-time

🕸️ What is LangGraph? Beginner
LangGraph extends LangChain with a stateful, graph-based framework for building complex, cyclical AI workflows.
beginnerlanggraph
▼
🕸️
Key InsightLangGraph models agent behavior as a directed graph where nodes are functions/LLM calls and edges are transitions. This enables loops, branching, and parallelism — impossible with simple chains.
Core Building Blocks
State
TypedDict that persists across all nodes. Every node reads & writes to it.
Node
A Python function (or LLM call) that takes state and returns state updates.
Edge
Connection between nodes. Can be conditional (if/else routing logic).
Graph
The compiled workflow. Has START and END special nodes.
Checkpointer
Persists state snapshots for resume, time-travel, and human-in-the-loop.
Reducer
How state fields are merged when multiple nodes update the same key.
LangGraph vs. LangChain Chains
✅ LangGraph (Graph)
  • Cycles & loops
  • Persistent state across turns
  • Conditional routing
  • Parallel node execution
  • Human-in-the-loop
  • Time-travel debugging
⚠️ LangChain Chains (LCEL)
  • Simple linear pipelines
  • Great for one-shot RAG
  • No native cycles
  • Limited state persistence
  • No conditional branching
📖 Key Terminology Glossary Beginner
Essential terms you'll encounter when building RAG systems and AI agents.
beginner
▼
TermDefinition
EmbeddingNumerical vector representation of text. Similar texts have similar vectors.
ChunkA segment of a document. RAG indexes and retrieves at chunk granularity.
Vector StoreDatabase optimized for similarity search over embeddings (e.g., Chroma, Pinecone, Qdrant).
Context WindowMax tokens an LLM can process at once (e.g., GPT-4o: 128K, Claude 3.5: 200K).
TemperatureLLM randomness. 0 = deterministic, 1 = creative. Use 0 for factual agent tasks.
Tool CallingLLM structured ability to invoke external functions/APIs. Core to agents.
ReActReason + Act pattern: Think → Act → Observe → repeat.
GroundingConnecting LLM outputs to verifiable, real sources of truth.
HallucinationLLM generating plausible-sounding but false information.
Graph StateTypedDict schema that flows through LangGraph nodes.
CheckpointerLangGraph mechanism to save/restore state snapshots (enables memory & HITL).
SupervisorAgent that routes subtasks to specialist agents in multi-agent systems.
ReducerFunction defining how state field updates are merged (e.g., append to list).
RunnableLangChain abstraction for anything callable via .invoke()/.stream()/.batch().
🦙 Ollama Setup
🦙 What is Ollama? Why Use It? Beginner
Run powerful LLMs locally — no API key, no cost, no data leaving your machine.
beginnerollamalocal
▼
🦙
Ollama in one sentenceOllama is a tool that lets you download and run open-source LLMs (Llama 3, Mistral, Gemma, Phi, etc.) on your own computer — works on Mac, Linux, and Windows. Perfect for learning without API costs.
Ollama vs Cloud APIs
✅ Ollama (Local)
  • 100% free — no API costs
  • No data leaves your machine
  • Works fully offline
  • No rate limits
  • Great for development & testing
  • Slower on CPU (need good GPU)
  • Smaller models than GPT-4o
⚠️ Cloud APIs (OpenAI, etc.)
  • State-of-the-art models
  • Fast inference
  • No hardware required
  • Costs money per token
  • Data sent to provider
  • Rate limits apply
Recommended Hardware
Model SizeMin RAMGPU VRAMSpeed (CPU)
1–3B params (phi3, gemma2:2b)8 GB4 GBFast ✅
7–8B params (llama3.1, mistral)16 GB8 GBUsable 🟡
13B params32 GB12 GBSlow 🔴
70B params (llama3.3)64 GB40 GBVery slow ❌
💡
Best starter modelFor learning on a laptop: llama3.2:3b (fast, smart enough) or phi3:mini (Microsoft's efficient small model). For a desktop with good GPU: llama3.1:8b or mistral:7b.
⚙️ Ollama Installation & CLI Commands Beginner
Install Ollama and master the essential CLI commands for managing and running local models.
beginnercodeollamalocal
▼
Install Ollama
bash
# macOS / Linux — one-liner
curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh

# macOS — via Homebrew
brew install ollama

# Windows — download installer at: https://ollama.com/download

# Verify installation
ollama --version
Essential CLI Commands
bash
# ── Download & run a model ────────────────────────────────
ollama pull llama3.2          # Download llama3.2:latest
ollama pull llama3.2:3b       # Specific size variant
ollama pull mistral           # Mistral 7B
ollama pull phi3:mini         # Microsoft Phi-3 mini (very fast)
ollama pull nomic-embed-text  # Embedding model (for RAG!)
ollama pull mxbai-embed-large # High-quality embedding model
ollama pull codellama         # Code-specialized model
ollama pull gemma2:9b         # Google Gemma 2

# ── Run a model interactively ─────────────────────────────
ollama run llama3.2           # Chat in terminal
ollama run mistral "Explain RAG in 3 sentences"  # One-shot

# ── Manage models ─────────────────────────────────────────
ollama list                   # Show downloaded models
ollama show llama3.2          # Show model info/parameters
ollama rm llama3.2            # Delete a model
ollama cp llama3.2 my-model   # Copy/rename model

# ── Server control ────────────────────────────────────────
ollama serve                  # Start server (auto-starts on install)
# Server runs on http://localhost:11434 by default

# ── REST API (Ollama exposes OpenAI-compatible API) ───────
curl http://localhost:11434/api/generate -d '{
  "model": "llama3.2",
  "prompt": "What is RAG?",
  "stream": false
}'

# Check running models
curl http://localhost:11434/api/ps
Recommended Models for Learning
ModelSizeBest ForPull Command
llama3.2:3b2 GBFast general chat, low RAMollama pull llama3.2:3b
llama3.1:8b4.7 GBBest open general LLM ✅ollama pull llama3.1:8b
mistral:7b4.1 GBFast, great instruction followingollama pull mistral
phi3:mini2.3 GBFastest, good reasoningollama pull phi3:mini
codellama:7b3.8 GBCode generationollama pull codellama
nomic-embed-text274 MBRAG embeddings (free!) ✅ollama pull nomic-embed-text
mxbai-embed-large669 MBHigher accuracy embeddingsollama pull mxbai-embed-large
gemma2:9b5.5 GBGoogle model, great qualityollama pull gemma2:9b
🎭 Ollama Modelfile — Custom Personas Intermediate
Create custom models with system prompts, temperature, and parameter tuning baked in.
intermediatecodeollama
▼
bash
# Create a file called Modelfile (no extension)
cat > Modelfile << 'EOF'
FROM llama3.1:8b

# System prompt baked into the model
SYSTEM """
You are an expert AI/RAG assistant specializing in LangGraph.
Always provide working Python code examples.
Be concise and practical — no fluff.
When explaining concepts, use simple analogies first.
"""

# Parameters
PARAMETER temperature 0.1        # Lower = more deterministic
PARAMETER top_p 0.9
PARAMETER top_k 40
PARAMETER num_ctx 8192            # Context window size
PARAMETER repeat_penalty 1.1     # Reduce repetition

# Optional: custom template
TEMPLATE """{{ if .System }}<|system|>
{{ .System }}<|end|>
{{ end }}{{ if .Prompt }}<|user|>
{{ .Prompt }}<|end|>
<|assistant|>
{{ end }}{{ .Response }}<|end|>"""
EOF

# Build and create the model
ollama create rag-assistant -f Modelfile

# Run your custom model
ollama run rag-assistant "How do I build a RAG pipeline?"

# List to see it
ollama list
💡
Tip for RAG projectsSet temperature 0 for factual Q&A agents. Use temperature 0.7 for creative writing agents. Always increase num_ctx to at least 4096 when using RAG — small context windows will truncate your retrieved documents.
⚙️ Environment Setup
📦 Installation & Dependencies Beginner
Complete dependency setup for RAG + LangGraph projects — core, vector stores, LLMs, and observability.

▼
Core Packages
bash
# Core LangGraph & LangChain
pip install langgraph langchain langchain-core langchain-community

# LLM providers (choose one or many)
pip install langchain-openai        # GPT-4o, o1
pip install langchain-anthropic     # Claude 3.5
pip install langchain-google-genai  # Gemini
pip install langchain-ollama        # Local models via Ollama

# Embeddings
pip install langchain-openai        # text-embedding-3-small/large
pip install sentence-transformers   # Open-source embeddings

# Vector stores
pip install chromadb                # Local, great for dev
pip install pinecone-client         # Production cloud
pip install qdrant-client           # Enterprise self-hosted
pip install faiss-cpu               # Facebook's fast in-memory

# Document loaders & processing
pip install pypdf docx2txt          # PDF, Word
pip install unstructured            # Multi-format (HTML, PPTX, etc.)
pip install tiktoken                # Token counting

# LangGraph server & streaming
pip install "langgraph[cli]"        # langgraph dev server
pip install langgraph-checkpoint-postgres  # Production checkpointing

# Observability
pip install langsmith               # LangSmith tracing
pip install opentelemetry-api       # OTel for enterprise

# Development utilities
pip install python-dotenv           # .env management
pip install pydantic>=2             # Data validation
Environment Variables (.env)
bash
# LLM API Keys
OPENAI_API_KEY=sk-...
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=sk-ant-...
GOOGLE_API_KEY=...

# LangSmith (observability - highly recommended)
LANGCHAIN_TRACING_V2=true
LANGCHAIN_API_KEY=lsv2_...
LANGCHAIN_PROJECT=my-rag-agent

# Vector store (if using cloud)
PINECONE_API_KEY=...
PINECONE_ENVIRONMENT=us-east1-gcp

# Database (for production checkpointing)
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@host:5432/langgraph

🧠 LangChain + Ollama Integration
🔗 LangChain + Ollama — First Integration Beginner
Connect LangChain to your local Ollama models — drop-in replacement for OpenAI.
beginnercodeollamalangchainlocal
▼
✅
Make sure Ollama is runningBefore running Python code, start Ollama: open a terminal and run ollama serve (or it auto-starts on Mac after install). Verify with curl http://localhost:11434/api/tags.
bash
pip install langchain-ollama langchain-core
python
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama, OllamaEmbeddings

# ── Chat Model ────────────────────────────────────────────
llm = ChatOllama(
    model="llama3.2",          # Any model you've pulled
    temperature=0,             # 0 = deterministic (good for RAG)
    base_url="http://localhost:11434",  # Default Ollama URL
    num_ctx=4096,              # Context window
    num_predict=512,           # Max tokens to generate
)

# Invoke exactly like ChatOpenAI — same interface!
response = llm.invoke("What is Retrieval-Augmented Generation?")
print(response.content)

# Streaming
for chunk in llm.stream("Explain LangGraph in 3 bullet points"):
    print(chunk.content, end="", flush=True)

# With messages (chat history)
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, SystemMessage, AIMessage
messages = [
    SystemMessage(content="You are an expert Python developer."),
    HumanMessage(content="Write a hello world in Python."),
]
response = llm.invoke(messages)
print(response.content)

# ── Embeddings ────────────────────────────────────────────
embeddings = OllamaEmbeddings(
    model="nomic-embed-text",  # or "mxbai-embed-large"
    base_url="http://localhost:11434",
)

# Embed a single text
vector = embeddings.embed_query("What is RAG?")
print(f"Vector dimension: {len(vector)}")  # 768 for nomic-embed-text

# Embed multiple documents
vectors = embeddings.embed_documents(["Doc 1 text", "Doc 2 text"])
print(f"Embedded {len(vectors)} documents")
Switching Between Providers (Zero Code Change)
python
import os

# Use environment variable to switch provider
USE_LOCAL = os.getenv("USE_LOCAL_LLM", "true").lower() == "true"

if USE_LOCAL:
    from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama, OllamaEmbeddings
    llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1:8b", temperature=0)
    embeddings = OllamaEmbeddings(model="nomic-embed-text")
    print("🦙 Using local Ollama models")
else:
    from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI, OpenAIEmbeddings
    llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o-mini", temperature=0)
    embeddings = OpenAIEmbeddings(model="text-embedding-3-small")
    print("☁️  Using OpenAI API")

# The rest of your code is IDENTICAL regardless of provider
response = llm.invoke("Explain RAG briefly.")

🔸 Gemini Setup — Install & Authenticate Intermediate
Get your Gemini API key from Google AI Studio, install the LangChain integration, and verify the connection in under 2 minutes.

▼
Step 1 — Get your API key
WhereURL

Google Cloud Vertex AI (enterprise, IAM auth)console.cloud.google.com/vertex-ai
💡
Which to use? — If you have a Gemini Pro subscription through Google AI Studio, use GOOGLE_API_KEY from AI Studio. Vertex AI is for Google Cloud projects with IAM service accounts.

Step 4 — Quick smoke test
python
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash")   # fast + cheap
response = llm.invoke("Say hello in one sentence.")

# → "Hello! It's great to meet you."

🔄 Gemini as a Drop-in Replacement for Ollama Intermediate
LangChain's unified interface means you only change one line to switch between Ollama and Gemini — every chain, agent, and graph works identically.
intermediategeminilangchain
▼
python
import os
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI, GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama, OllamaEmbeddings

# ── Choose your backend via env var ──────────────────────────────────
USE_GEMINI = os.getenv("LLM_BACKEND", "ollama").lower() == "gemini"

if USE_GEMINI:
    llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
        model="gemini-2.0-flash",
        temperature=0.2,
        max_output_tokens=8192,
    )
    embeddings = GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings(
        model="models/text-embedding-004"   # 768-dim, free tier included
    )
else:
    llm        = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1", temperature=0.2)
    embeddings = OllamaEmbeddings(model="nomic-embed-text")

# ── Everything below works with BOTH backends unchanged ───────────────
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate

prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a concise assistant."),
    ("human",  "{question}"),
])
chain = prompt | llm

print(chain.invoke({"question": "What is RAG?"}).content)
💡
Embed dimension mismatch — if you switch from Ollama embeddings to Gemini embeddings, you must re-index your vector store. The embedding dimensions differ (Ollama nomic-embed-text = 768, Gemini text-embedding-004 = 768 — same! But older Ollama models vary). Always check before reusing an existing FAISS index.
🔍 RAG Pipeline with Gemini + FAISS Intermediate
Full local RAG using Gemini for both the LLM and embeddings, with FAISS as the vector store — no Ollama required.
intermediategeminirag
▼
bash
pip install langchain-google-genai faiss-cpu langchain-community
python
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI, GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_community.document_loaders import TextLoader
from langchain_text_splitters import RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnablePassthrough
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser

# ── Models ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
llm        = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash", temperature=0)
embeddings = GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings(model="models/text-embedding-004")

# ── Index documents ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
loader    = TextLoader("your_document.txt")
docs      = loader.load()
splitter  = RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter(chunk_size=1000, chunk_overlap=200)
chunks    = splitter.split_documents(docs)
vectorstore = FAISS.from_documents(chunks, embeddings)
vectorstore.save_local("faiss_gemini_index")   # persist to disk

retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 4})

# ── RAG chain ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Answer the question using only the context below.
If the answer is not in the context, say "I don't have that information."

Context:
{context}

Question: {question}
""")

rag_chain = (
    {"context": retriever, "question": RunnablePassthrough()}
    | prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)

answer = rag_chain.invoke("What does the document say about X?")
print(answer)

# ── Load persisted index later ────────────────────────────────────────
vectorstore2 = FAISS.load_local(
    "faiss_gemini_index", embeddings,
    allow_dangerous_deserialization=True
)
🤖 LangGraph Agent with Gemini Intermediate
Stateful ReAct agent using LangGraph and Gemini's native function-calling — Gemini Pro is excellent at tool use.
intermediategeminilanggraphagent
▼
python
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langgraph.prebuilt import create_react_agent
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
    model="gemini-2.5-pro",    # best tool-use performance
    temperature=0,
)

# ── Define tools ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@tool
def calculator(expression: str) -> str:
    """Evaluate a mathematical expression. Input: a valid Python math expression."""
    try:
        return str(eval(expression, {"__builtins__": {}}, {}))
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Error: {e}"

@tool
def get_weather(city: str) -> str:
    """Get the current weather for a city (mock — replace with real API)."""
    return f"The weather in {city} is 22°C and sunny."

tools = [calculator, get_weather]

# ── Build agent ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
memory = MemorySaver()
agent  = create_react_agent(llm, tools, checkpointer=memory)

# ── Run with thread (persistent memory across turns) ──────────────────
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "user-123"}}

result = agent.invoke(
    {"messages": [("user", "What is 247 * 19? Also, what's the weather in London?")]},
    config=config,
)
print(result["messages"][-1].content)

# ── Follow-up turn (remembers the conversation) ───────────────────────
result2 = agent.invoke(
    {"messages": [("user", "Double the result of the calculation you just did.")]},
    config=config,   # same thread_id → same memory
)
print(result2["messages"][-1].content)
💡
Gemini function calling — Gemini natively supports parallel function calls (calling multiple tools in one turn). LangChain's tool binding handles this automatically when you use create_react_agent or llm.bind_tools(tools).
📷 Gemini Multimodal — Images, PDFs & Audio Advanced
Gemini 2.0 Flash and 2.5 Pro are natively multimodal — send images, PDFs, and audio directly via LangChain's message format.
advancedgeminimultimodal
▼
Analyse an image (URL or local file)
python
import base64
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash")

# ── Option A: image from URL ──────────────────────────────────────────
msg = HumanMessage(content=[
    {"type": "text",      "text": "Describe this image in detail."},
    {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": "https://example.com/chart.png"}},
])
print(llm.invoke([msg]).content)

# ── Option B: local image as base64 ──────────────────────────────────
with open("diagram.png", "rb") as f:
    img_b64 = base64.b64encode(f.read()).decode()

msg2 = HumanMessage(content=[
    {"type": "text", "text": "What does this architecture diagram show?"},
    {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {
        "url": f"data:image/png;base64,{img_b64}"
    }},
])
print(llm.invoke([msg2]).content)
Analyse a PDF (Gemini handles up to 300 pages natively)
python
import base64
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.5-pro")

with open("report.pdf", "rb") as f:
    pdf_b64 = base64.b64encode(f.read()).decode()

msg = HumanMessage(content=[
    {"type": "text", "text": "Summarise the key findings in this report."},
    {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {
        "url": f"data:application/pdf;base64,{pdf_b64}"
    }},
])
print(llm.invoke([msg]).content)
Multimodal RAG — extract text from images then index it
python
from pathlib import Path
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI, GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_core.documents import Document
import base64

llm        = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash")
embeddings = GoogleGenerativeAIEmbeddings(model="models/text-embedding-004")

def image_to_text(path: str) -> str:
    """Use Gemini vision to extract text/data from an image."""
    b64 = base64.b64encode(Path(path).read_bytes()).decode()
    msg = HumanMessage(content=[
        {"type": "text", "text": "Extract all text, tables, and data from this image. Be thorough."},
        {"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": f"data:image/png;base64,{b64}"}},
    ])
    return llm.invoke([msg]).content

# Extract text from all images, then index
image_paths = ["slide1.png", "slide2.png", "chart.png"]
documents   = [
    Document(page_content=image_to_text(p), metadata={"source": p})
    for p in image_paths
]
vectorstore = FAISS.from_documents(documents, embeddings)
retriever   = vectorstore.as_retriever()
💦 Gemini Streaming & Long Context Tips Advanced
Stream tokens as they arrive, handle rate limits gracefully, and make the most of Gemini's 1M+ token context window.
advancedgeministreaming
▼
Streaming
python
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash", streaming=True)

# Sync streaming
for chunk in llm.stream("Write a short poem about RAG systems."):
    print(chunk.content, end="", flush=True)

# Async streaming (for FastAPI / Streamlit)
async def stream_response(question: str):
    async for chunk in llm.astream(question):
        yield chunk.content

# Streamlit integration
import streamlit as st
st.write_stream(llm.stream("Explain LangGraph in 3 bullet points."))
Long context — send an entire codebase or book
python
from pathlib import Path
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, SystemMessage

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
    model="gemini-2.5-pro",   # 1M token context
    temperature=0.1,
)

# Load a large document (book, codebase, transcripts)
large_text = Path("entire_codebase.txt").read_text()   # e.g. 500K tokens

messages = [
    SystemMessage(content="You are a senior code reviewer."),
    HumanMessage(content=f"Review this codebase and list all security issues:\n\n{large_text}"),
]
response = llm.invoke(messages)
print(response.content)
Rate limit handling
python
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from tenacity import retry, stop_after_attempt, wait_exponential, retry_if_exception_type
import google.api_core.exceptions as gexc

llm = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash")

@retry(
    retry=retry_if_exception_type((gexc.ResourceExhausted, gexc.ServiceUnavailable)),
    wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=2, min=2, max=60),
    stop=stop_after_attempt(5),
)
def invoke_with_retry(text: str):
    return llm.invoke(text)

result = invoke_with_retry("What is the capital of France?")
print(result.content)

⚖️ Gemini vs Ollama — When to Use Which Intermediate
A practical decision guide for choosing between Gemini (cloud) and Ollama (local) based on your task, privacy needs, and hardware.
intermediategeminiarchitecture
▼
FactorGemini (cloud)Ollama (local)
Quality✅ Frontier model quality, best-in-class reasoning🔷 Good — 70B models rival GPT-4, smaller are fast
Speed🔷 ~50–200 tok/s depending on model and load🔷 ~20–80 tok/s on GPU; slow on CPU
Cost⚠️ Pay-per-token (free tier exists; Pro sub included)✅ Free after hardware cost
Privacy❌ Data sent to Google servers✅ 100% local — nothing leaves your machine
Context window✅ 1M–2M tokens (entire codebases, books)🔷 8K–128K depending on model
Multimodal✅ Images, PDFs, audio natively🔷 Only vision-capable models (llava, etc.)
Internet access✅ Works anywhere with internet🔷 Local only (no internet needed)
Offline use❌ Requires internet✅ Works offline
Setup✅ API key → works immediately🔷 Install Ollama + pull model (5 mins)
Compliance❌ Hard for HIPAA/GDPR without DPA✅ Easy — data stays on-premises
Recommended hybrid pattern
python
import os
from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama

def get_llm(task: str):
    """Route to the right model based on the task."""
    if task in ("vision", "long_doc", "complex_reasoning"):
        # Use Gemini for tasks that need frontier quality or multimodal
        return ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.5-pro", temperature=0)

    elif task in ("rag_qa", "summarise", "extract"):
        # Use Gemini Flash for fast, cheap everyday tasks
        return ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(model="gemini-2.0-flash", temperature=0)

    else:
        # Use Ollama for private data, offline use, or high-volume cheap tasks
        return ChatOllama(model="llama3.1", temperature=0)

# Development → local Ollama (free, private)
# Production sensitive data → Ollama on-prem
# Production public data → Gemini Flash (fast, cheap)
# Complex one-off tasks → Gemini 2.5 Pro
⛓️ LangChain Core Concepts
⛓️ LangChain Architecture Overview Beginner
Understand the key LangChain building blocks and how they compose into pipelines.
beginnerlangchain
▼
🤖 Chat Models
The LLM interface. ChatOpenAI, ChatOllama, ChatAnthropic. All share the same .invoke() API.
💬 Prompt Templates
Reusable, parameterized prompts. ChatPromptTemplate, PromptTemplate, MessagesPlaceholder.
📤 Output Parsers
Transform raw LLM text into structured data. StrOutputParser, JsonOutputParser, PydanticOutputParser.
🔍 Retrievers
Fetch relevant documents from any source. Vector stores, BM25, web search — all expose the same .invoke() interface.
🔢 Embeddings
Convert text to vectors. OllamaEmbeddings, OpenAIEmbeddings, HuggingFaceEmbeddings.
🗄️ Vector Stores
Store and search embeddings. Chroma, FAISS, Qdrant, Pinecone — all have .as_retriever().
🔧 Tools
Functions the LLM can call. Decorated with @tool or created from StructuredTool.
📄 Document Loaders
Load files into Document objects. PDF, Word, Web, CSV, JSON, Notion, GitHub and 100+ more.
The Runnable Interface (Everything is Composable)
💡
Key design principleEvery LangChain component (LLM, prompt, parser, retriever) is a Runnable. Runnables share 4 methods: .invoke(), .stream(), .batch(), .ainvoke(). You can pipe them with | to form chains.
python
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.2", temperature=0)

# Every component is a Runnable — all have .invoke()
prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Explain {topic} in one sentence.")
parser = StrOutputParser()

# Pipe with | to create a chain (LCEL)
chain = prompt | llm | parser

# .invoke() — single input, blocks until done
result = chain.invoke({"topic": "RAG"})

# .stream() — yields tokens as they arrive
for token in chain.stream({"topic": "LangGraph"}):
    print(token, end="", flush=True)

# .batch() — process multiple inputs in parallel
results = chain.batch([{"topic": "RAG"}, {"topic": "Agents"}, {"topic": "Embeddings"}])

# .ainvoke() — async version for FastAPI / async code
import asyncio
result = asyncio.run(chain.ainvoke({"topic": "RAG"}))
💬 Prompt Templates — All Patterns Beginner
Master every type of prompt template for chat models, few-shot examples, and dynamic message injection.
beginnercodelangchain
▼
python
from langchain_core.prompts import (
    ChatPromptTemplate,
    PromptTemplate,
    MessagesPlaceholder,
    FewShotChatMessagePromptTemplate,
)
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, AIMessage

# ── 1. Simple string prompt ───────────────────────────────
simple = PromptTemplate.from_template("What is {topic}?")
print(simple.invoke({"topic": "RAG"}).text)

# ── 2. Chat prompt (most common) ──────────────────────────
chat_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a {role}. Answer in {language}."),
    ("human", "{question}"),
])
result = chat_prompt.invoke({
    "role": "Python expert",
    "language": "English",
    "question": "What is a generator?",
})

# ── 3. Inject chat history (for multi-turn agents) ────────
history_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a helpful assistant."),
    MessagesPlaceholder("chat_history"),  # Inject list of messages here
    ("human", "{question}"),
])

chat_history = [
    HumanMessage(content="My name is Alice."),
    AIMessage(content="Nice to meet you, Alice!"),
]

messages = history_prompt.invoke({
    "chat_history": chat_history,
    "question": "What is my name?"
})

# ── 4. Few-shot prompting (show examples to the model) ────
examples = [
    {"input": "happy",   "output": "sad"},
    {"input": "tall",    "output": "short"},
    {"input": "fast",    "output": "slow"},
]

example_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("human", "What is the antonym of: {input}"),
    ("ai", "{output}"),
])

few_shot = FewShotChatMessagePromptTemplate(
    example_prompt=example_prompt,
    examples=examples,
)

final_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You find antonyms."),
    few_shot,               # Examples injected here
    ("human", "What is the antonym of: {word}"),
])

chain = final_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()
print(chain.invoke({"word": "bright"}))  # → "dim"

# ── 5. Partial prompts (bake in fixed values) ─────────────
partial_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template(
    "Summarize this {document} in {language}."
).partial(language="English")   # language is now fixed

# Only needs 'document' at invoke time
result = (partial_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()).invoke(
    {"document": "LangGraph is a graph-based agent framework..."}
)
📤 Output Parsers — Structured Outputs Beginner
Extract structured data from LLM responses — JSON, Pydantic models, lists, and more.
beginnercodelangchain
▼
python
from langchain_core.output_parsers import (
    StrOutputParser,       # → plain string
    JsonOutputParser,      # → dict/list
    CommaSeparatedListOutputParser,  # → list of strings
)
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from typing import List

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1:8b", temperature=0)

# ── 1. String parser (most common) ───────────────────────
chain = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Answer: {q}") | llm | StrOutputParser()
text = chain.invoke({"q": "What is 2+2?"})   # → "4"

# ── 2. JSON parser ────────────────────────────────────────
json_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Extract entities from this text as JSON with keys: "people", "places", "dates"
Text: {text}
Return ONLY valid JSON, no explanation.
""")
json_chain = json_prompt | llm | JsonOutputParser()
result = json_chain.invoke({"text": "Alice visited Paris in 2024."})
# → {"people": ["Alice"], "places": ["Paris"], "dates": ["2024"]}

# ── 3. Pydantic parser (type-safe, validated) ─────────────
class MovieReview(BaseModel):
    title: str = Field(description="Movie title")
    rating: float = Field(description="Rating from 0 to 10")
    pros: List[str] = Field(description="List of positives")
    cons: List[str] = Field(description="List of negatives")
    recommend: bool = Field(description="Whether to recommend")

# with_structured_output() is the cleanest approach
structured_llm = llm.with_structured_output(MovieReview)

review = structured_llm.invoke(
    "Review the movie Inception briefly."
)
print(f"Rating: {review.rating}")
print(f"Recommend: {review.recommend}")
print(f"Pros: {review.pros}")

# ── 4. Comma separated list parser ───────────────────────
list_chain = (
    ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("List 5 {category} in a comma-separated list.")
    | llm
    | CommaSeparatedListOutputParser()
)
items = list_chain.invoke({"category": "Python frameworks"})
# → ["Django", "FastAPI", "Flask", "Tornado", "Sanic"]

# ── 5. Custom parser with RunnableLambda ──────────────────
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableLambda

def parse_numbered_list(text: str) -> list:
    """Parse '1. item\n2. item' format."""
    lines = text.strip().split('\n')
    return [line.split('. ', 1)[1] for line in lines if '. ' in line]

custom_chain = (
    ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("List 3 {topic} numbered.")
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
    | RunnableLambda(parse_numbered_list)
)
items = custom_chain.invoke({"topic": "benefits of RAG"})
📚 RAG Basics
✂️ Document Loading & Chunking Beginner
Load documents from various sources and split them into chunks suitable for embedding.
beginnercoderag
▼
Loading Different Document Types
python
from langchain_community.document_loaders import (
    PyPDFLoader,          # PDF files
    TextLoader,           # Plain text
    WebBaseLoader,        # Web pages
    DirectoryLoader,      # All files in a folder
    CSVLoader,            # CSV files
    JSONLoader,           # JSON files
    UnstructuredMarkdownLoader,
)

# Load a PDF
loader = PyPDFLoader("docs/report.pdf")
docs = loader.load()  # Returns List[Document]

# Load all PDFs in a directory
dir_loader = DirectoryLoader("./data/", glob="**/*.pdf", loader_cls=PyPDFLoader)
docs = dir_loader.load()

# Load from a URL
web_loader = WebBaseLoader(["https://docs.langchain.com/"])
docs = web_loader.load()

# Each Document has:
# doc.page_content  -> the text
# doc.metadata      -> {"source": "...", "page": 0, ...}
Chunking Strategies
python
from langchain.text_splitter import (
    RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter,  # ✅ Best default
    TokenTextSplitter,               # Token-aware splitting
    MarkdownHeaderTextSplitter,      # Respects MD headers
    HTMLHeaderTextSplitter,          # Respects HTML structure
    SemanticChunker,                 # Semantically coherent chunks
)

# ✅ Recommended: RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter
splitter = RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter(
    chunk_size=1000,        # Characters per chunk
    chunk_overlap=200,      # Overlap prevents context loss at boundaries
    separators=["\n\n", "\n", ". ", " ", ""],  # Try these in order
    add_start_index=True,   # Adds metadata.start_index
)
chunks = splitter.split_documents(docs)
print(f"Split {len(docs)} docs into {len(chunks)} chunks")

# Token-based splitting (more precise for LLM context limits)
token_splitter = TokenTextSplitter(
    chunk_size=500,        # Tokens per chunk
    chunk_overlap=50,
    encoding_name="cl100k_base",  # Same as GPT-4
)

# Semantic chunking (experimental, uses embeddings)
from langchain_experimental.text_splitter import SemanticChunker
from langchain_openai import OpenAIEmbeddings
semantic_splitter = SemanticChunker(OpenAIEmbeddings())
semantic_chunks = semantic_splitter.split_documents(docs)
💡
Chunking Rules of Thumbchunk_size: 500-1000 chars for QA. 2000+ for summarization. chunk_overlap: ~20% of chunk_size prevents information loss at boundaries. Always preserve metadata (source, page) for citations.

Embed your chunks and store them in a vector database for semantic retrieval.

Setup with Chroma (Local Dev)
python
from langchain_openai import OpenAIEmbeddings
from langchain_chroma import Chroma

# Initialize embedding model
embeddings = OpenAIEmbeddings(model="text-embedding-3-small")

# Create vector store from documents
vectorstore = Chroma.from_documents(
    documents=chunks,
    embedding=embeddings,
    collection_name="my_docs",
    persist_directory="./chroma_db",   # Saves to disk
)

# Or load existing store
vectorstore = Chroma(
    collection_name="my_docs",
    embedding_function=embeddings,
    persist_directory="./chroma_db",
)

# ---- Retrieval ----
# Basic similarity search
results = vectorstore.similarity_search("What is RAG?", k=4)

# With relevance scores (0=perfect, 1=dissimilar)
results = vectorstore.similarity_search_with_relevance_scores("What is RAG?", k=4)

# As a retriever (most common usage)
retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(
    search_type="mmr",          # Maximal Marginal Relevance (diversity)
    search_kwargs={"k": 6, "fetch_k": 20}
)
Pinecone (Production Cloud)
python
from pinecone import Pinecone, ServerlessSpec
from langchain_pinecone import PineconeVectorStore

pc = Pinecone(api_key=os.getenv("PINECONE_API_KEY"))

# Create index (once)
pc.create_index(
    name="my-rag-index",
    dimension=1536,
    metric="cosine",
    spec=ServerlessSpec(cloud="aws", region="us-east-1"),
)

index = pc.Index("my-rag-index")
vectorstore = PineconeVectorStore(index=index, embedding=embeddings)
⛓️ Simple RAG Chain (LCEL) Beginner
Build your first working RAG pipeline in under 20 lines using LangChain Expression Language.
beginnercoderag
▼
python
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI, OpenAIEmbeddings
from langchain_chroma import Chroma
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnablePassthrough

# 1. Setup components
llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o-mini", temperature=0)
embeddings = OpenAIEmbeddings(model="text-embedding-3-small")
vectorstore = Chroma(persist_directory="./chroma_db", embedding_function=embeddings)
retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 4})

# 2. RAG Prompt
RAG_PROMPT = """You are an assistant for question-answering tasks.
Use ONLY the following retrieved context to answer the question.
If you don't know the answer, say "I don't have enough information."
Cite your sources by mentioning the document source.

Context:
{context}

Question: {question}

Answer:"""

prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template(RAG_PROMPT)

# 3. Format retrieved docs
def format_docs(docs):
    return "\n\n".join(
        f"Source: {doc.metadata.get('source', 'unknown')}\n{doc.page_content}"
        for doc in docs
    )

# 4. Build the chain (LCEL pipe syntax)
rag_chain = (
    {"context": retriever | format_docs, "question": RunnablePassthrough()}
    | prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)

# 5. Invoke
answer = rag_chain.invoke("What are the main benefits of RAG?")
print(answer)

# With streaming
for chunk in rag_chain.stream("What are the main benefits of RAG?"):
    print(chunk, end="", flush=True)
✅
This is the foundation. Once this works, you'll refactor it into a LangGraph node for more complex workflows.
📦 Fully Local RAG (100% Free, No API Key)
📦 Build a Fully Local RAG Pipeline Beginner
Complete end-to-end RAG with zero API costs: Ollama LLM + Ollama Embeddings + Chroma vector store.
beginnercodeollamaraglangchainlocal
▼
🎯
Prerequisites (all free)1. ollama pull llama3.1:8b — your LLM. 2. ollama pull nomic-embed-text — your embedding model. 3. pip install langchain-ollama langchain-chroma langchain-community pypdf
python
"""
100% Local RAG Pipeline
No API keys required. No internet needed after setup.
"""

from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama, OllamaEmbeddings
from langchain_chroma import Chroma
from langchain_community.document_loaders import PyPDFLoader, WebBaseLoader
from langchain.text_splitter import RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnablePassthrough

# ── Step 1: Load Documents ────────────────────────────────
print("📄 Loading documents...")

# Load from PDF
loader = PyPDFLoader("your_document.pdf")
docs = loader.load()

# OR load from URL
# loader = WebBaseLoader("https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrieval-augmented_generation")
# docs = loader.load()

# OR create sample docs for testing
from langchain_core.documents import Document
docs = [
    Document(page_content="LangGraph is a library for building stateful, multi-actor applications with LLMs.", metadata={"source": "intro.txt"}),
    Document(page_content="RAG stands for Retrieval-Augmented Generation. It combines retrieval with generation.", metadata={"source": "rag.txt"}),
    Document(page_content="Ollama allows running LLMs locally. Key models: llama3, mistral, phi3.", metadata={"source": "ollama.txt"}),
]

# ── Step 2: Chunk Documents ───────────────────────────────
print("✂️  Chunking documents...")
splitter = RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter(
    chunk_size=500,
    chunk_overlap=100,
)
chunks = splitter.split_documents(docs)
print(f"   {len(docs)} docs → {len(chunks)} chunks")

# ── Step 3: Embed & Store (LOCAL embeddings!) ─────────────
print("🔢 Creating embeddings with Ollama...")
embeddings = OllamaEmbeddings(
    model="nomic-embed-text",   # Free, local, 768-dim
    base_url="http://localhost:11434",
)

vectorstore = Chroma.from_documents(
    documents=chunks,
    embedding=embeddings,
    collection_name="local_rag",
    persist_directory="./local_chroma_db",  # Saved to disk
)
print("✅ Vector store created!")

# ── Step 4: Create Retriever ──────────────────────────────
retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(
    search_type="similarity",
    search_kwargs={"k": 3},
)

# ── Step 5: Local LLM ─────────────────────────────────────
llm = ChatOllama(
    model="llama3.1:8b",
    temperature=0,
    num_ctx=4096,  # Important: set large enough for context + docs
)

# ── Step 6: RAG Prompt ────────────────────────────────────
RAG_PROMPT = """You are an assistant for question-answering tasks.
Use ONLY the following retrieved context to answer the question.
If the answer isn't in the context, say "I don't know based on the provided documents."
Keep your answer concise and cite sources when possible.

Context:
{context}

Question: {question}

Answer:"""

prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template(RAG_PROMPT)

# ── Step 7: Build Chain ───────────────────────────────────
def format_docs(docs):
    return "\n\n".join(
        f"[Source: {doc.metadata.get('source', 'unknown')}]\n{doc.page_content}"
        for doc in docs
    )

rag_chain = (
    {"context": retriever | format_docs, "question": RunnablePassthrough()}
    | prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)

# ── Step 8: Ask Questions! ────────────────────────────────
print("\n🦙 Local RAG is ready! Ask away...")
questions = [
    "What is LangGraph?",
    "What does RAG stand for?",
    "What models can Ollama run?",
]

for q in questions:
    print(f"\n❓ {q}")
    print(f"💬 {rag_chain.invoke(q)}")
Load Existing Vector Store (Skip Re-embedding)
python
import os

CHROMA_DIR = "./local_chroma_db"

if os.path.exists(CHROMA_DIR):
    print("📂 Loading existing vector store...")
    vectorstore = Chroma(
        collection_name="local_rag",
        embedding_function=embeddings,
        persist_directory=CHROMA_DIR,
    )
else:
    print("🔨 Creating new vector store...")
    vectorstore = Chroma.from_documents(chunks, embeddings, persist_directory=CHROMA_DIR)
🤖 Local LangGraph Agent with Ollama Intermediate
Build a fully local ReAct agent using Ollama + LangGraph — tools, memory, and all. Zero cost.
intermediatecodeollamalangchainlanggraphlocal
▼
⚠️
Tool calling with local modelsNot all Ollama models support reliable tool calling. Use: llama3.1:8b, llama3.2:3b, mistral-nemo, or qwen2.5:7b. Test with a simple tool before building complex agents.
python
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langgraph.prebuilt import create_react_agent
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

# ── Define Tools ──────────────────────────────────────────
@tool
def search_docs(query: str) -> str:
    """Search the local knowledge base for information.
    Use this for any question about internal documentation.
    Args:
        query: The search query
    """
    docs = retriever.invoke(query)
    if not docs:
        return "No relevant documents found."
    return "\n\n".join(
        f"Source: {d.metadata.get('source', '?')}\n{d.page_content}"
        for d in docs
    )

@tool
def calculator(expression: str) -> str:
    """Evaluate a simple math expression.
    Args:
        expression: Math expression like '15 * 200 / 100'
    """
    try:
        allowed = {k: v for k, v in __builtins__.items()
                   if k in ['abs', 'round', 'min', 'max', 'sum']} if isinstance(__builtins__, dict) else {}
        result = eval(expression, {"__builtins__": allowed})
        return f"Result: {result}"
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Math error: {e}"

@tool
def get_current_date() -> str:
    """Get today's date and time."""
    from datetime import datetime
    return f"Current date and time: {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}"

tools = [search_docs, calculator, get_current_date]

# ── Local LLM with tool support ───────────────────────────
local_llm = ChatOllama(
    model="llama3.1:8b",   # Must support tool calling
    temperature=0,
    num_ctx=8192,           # Larger context for tool results
)

# ── Build Agent ───────────────────────────────────────────
agent = create_react_agent(
    model=local_llm,
    tools=tools,
    checkpointer=MemorySaver(),
    state_modifier="""You are a helpful local assistant with access to a knowledge base.
    Use search_docs to find specific information before answering.
    Use calculator for any math. Use get_current_date if asked about time.
    Always base your answers on tool results when available."""
)

# ── Run ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "local-session-1"}}

def chat(question: str):
    result = agent.invoke(
        {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=question)]},
        config=config
    )
    return result["messages"][-1].content

print(chat("What does the knowledge base say about RAG?"))
print(chat("What is 15% of 2400?"))
print(chat("What is today's date?"))
🕸️ LangGraph Core
🕸️ Your First LangGraph Graph Beginner
Step-by-step walkthrough building a minimal, working LangGraph with state, nodes, and edges.
beginnercodelanggraph
▼
python
from typing import TypedDict, Annotated, List
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, AIMessage

# ── Step 1: Define State ──────────────────────────────────
class AgentState(TypedDict):
    # Annotated with add_messages = append to list (don't overwrite)
    messages: Annotated[List, add_messages]
    question: str
    answer: str

# ── Step 2: Define Nodes ──────────────────────────────────
llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o-mini", temperature=0)

def call_llm(state: AgentState) -> dict:
    """Node that calls the LLM."""
    response = llm.invoke(state["messages"])
    return {"messages": [response], "answer": response.content}

def format_output(state: AgentState) -> dict:
    """Node that formats the final answer."""
    return {"answer": f"Final: {state['answer']}"}

# ── Step 3: Build Graph ──────────────────────────────────
builder = StateGraph(AgentState)

# Add nodes
builder.add_node("llm_node", call_llm)
builder.add_node("format_node", format_output)

# Add edges (define execution order)
builder.add_edge(START, "llm_node")
builder.add_edge("llm_node", "format_node")
builder.add_edge("format_node", END)

# ── Step 4: Compile & Run ──────────────────────────────────
graph = builder.compile()

# Invoke
result = graph.invoke({
    "messages": [HumanMessage(content="What is 2+2?")],
    "question": "What is 2+2?",
    "answer": "",
})
print(result["answer"])

# Visualize (requires graphviz)
# print(graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid())
💡
Node Return Value RuleNodes return a dict with only the keys they want to update. LangGraph merges this dict into the existing state. Use add_messages reducer for message lists so they append instead of overwrite.
🔀 Conditional Edges & Routing Intermediate
Route between nodes dynamically based on state — the key to building decision-making agents.
intermediatecodelanggraph
▼
python
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from typing import TypedDict, Literal

class State(TypedDict):
    question: str
    needs_retrieval: bool
    retrieved_docs: list
    answer: str

# Router function — returns the name of the next node
def route_question(state: State) -> Literal["retrieve", "direct_answer"]:
    """Decide whether to retrieve context or answer directly."""
    question = state["question"].lower()
    
    # Simple keyword-based routing
    factual_keywords = ["what", "who", "when", "where", "how many"]
    if any(kw in question for kw in factual_keywords):
        return "retrieve"
    return "direct_answer"

def retrieve(state: State) -> dict:
    # ... vector search ...
    return {"retrieved_docs": ["doc1", "doc2"], "needs_retrieval": True}

def direct_answer(state: State) -> dict:
    return {"answer": "Based on general knowledge: ..."}

def rag_answer(state: State) -> dict:
    return {"answer": f"Based on {len(state['retrieved_docs'])} docs: ..."}

# --- Graph Setup ---
builder = StateGraph(State)
builder.add_node("retrieve", retrieve)
builder.add_node("direct_answer", direct_answer)
builder.add_node("rag_answer", rag_answer)

# Conditional edge: from START, call router to decide which node
builder.add_conditional_edges(
    START,              # Source node
    route_question,     # Router function
    {                   # Map return values to node names
        "retrieve": "retrieve",
        "direct_answer": "direct_answer",
    }
)

builder.add_edge("retrieve", "rag_answer")
builder.add_edge("direct_answer", END)
builder.add_edge("rag_answer", END)

graph = builder.compile()

# Test routing
result = graph.invoke({"question": "What is the capital of France?", "answer": "", "retrieved_docs": [], "needs_retrieval": False})
print(result["answer"])
📊 State & Memory
📊 State Schema Design Patterns Intermediate
Design robust state schemas with proper reducers for complex agent workflows.
intermediatecodememory
▼
python
from typing import TypedDict, Annotated, List, Optional, Any
from langchain_core.messages import BaseMessage
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
import operator

# ── Pattern 1: Basic State ───────────────────────────────
class BasicState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[List[BaseMessage], add_messages]  # Appends
    question: str       # Overwritten each update
    answer: str

# ── Pattern 2: Enterprise State ────────────────────────────
class EnterpriseState(TypedDict):
    # Conversation
    messages: Annotated[List[BaseMessage], add_messages]
    
    # Task tracking
    task_id: str
    current_step: str
    steps_taken: Annotated[List[str], operator.add]   # Appends
    
    # RAG
    query: str
    retrieved_docs: List[dict]
    context_quality_score: float
    
    # Agent decisions
    tool_calls: Annotated[List[dict], operator.add]
    should_retry: bool
    retry_count: int
    max_retries: int
    
    # Output
    final_answer: str
    sources: List[str]
    confidence: float
    
    # Metadata
    user_id: str
    session_id: str
    error: Optional[str]

# ── Pattern 3: Custom Reducer ──────────────────────────────
def keep_last_n(max_n: int):
    """Custom reducer: keep only the last N items."""
    def reducer(existing: list, new: list) -> list:
        combined = (existing or []) + (new or [])
        return combined[-max_n:]
    return reducer

class SlidingWindowState(TypedDict):
    # Only keep last 10 messages (memory management)
    messages: Annotated[List[BaseMessage], keep_last_n(10)]

# ── Pattern 4: Pydantic State (with validation) ───────────
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph

class ValidatedState(BaseModel):
    question: str = Field(description="User's question")
    answer: str = Field(default="")
    confidence: float = Field(default=0.0, ge=0.0, le=1.0)
    messages: List[Any] = Field(default_factory=list)

# Use with: builder = StateGraph(ValidatedState)
💾 Checkpointers — Persistent Memory Intermediate
Persist agent state across sessions with checkpointers. Essential for production multi-turn conversations.
intermediatecodememorylanggraph
▼
CheckpointerUse CasePackage
MemorySaverIn-memory (dev/testing only)langgraph
SqliteSaverSingle-server persistencelanggraph-checkpoint-sqlite
PostgresSaverProduction, multi-workerlanggraph-checkpoint-postgres
RedisSaverHigh-throughput, TTL supportlanggraph-checkpoint-redis
python
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

# ── Development: In-memory ───────────────────────────────
checkpointer = MemorySaver()
graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)

# thread_id identifies the conversation session
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "user-123-session-abc"}}

# First turn
result = graph.invoke(
    {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="My name is Alice.")]},
    config=config
)

# Second turn — graph remembers Alice!
result = graph.invoke(
    {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="What is my name?")]},
    config=config
)
print(result["messages"][-1].content)  # "Your name is Alice."

# ── Production: PostgreSQL ─────────────────────────────
from langgraph.checkpoint.postgres import PostgresSaver
import psycopg

conn_string = "postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/langgraph"

with PostgresSaver.from_conn_string(conn_string) as checkpointer:
    checkpointer.setup()  # Creates tables (first run only)
    graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)
    # Use as above...

# ── Time Travel: View past states ─────────────────────────
# List all checkpoints for a thread
for state in graph.get_state_history(config):
    print(f"Step: {state.metadata['step']}, Created: {state.created_at}")

# Rewind to a specific checkpoint
past_state = list(graph.get_state_history(config))[2]
rewound_result = graph.invoke(
    {"messages": [HumanMessage("Try again from here")]},
    config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "user-123", "checkpoint_id": past_state.config["configurable"]["checkpoint_id"]}}
)
🔧 Tools & Tool Calling
🔧 Defining Tools for Agents Intermediate
Create type-safe, well-documented tools that LLMs can call reliably.
intermediatecodetoolsagent
▼
python
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langchain.tools import StructuredTool
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from typing import Optional
import requests

# ── Method 1: @tool decorator (simplest) ─────────────────
@tool
def search_web(query: str) -> str:
    """Search the web for current information.
    
    Use this when you need real-time data, recent news, 
    or information the model may not have.
    
    Args:
        query: The search query string.
    """
    # Implementation (Tavily, SerpAPI, etc.)
    from langchain_community.tools.tavily_search import TavilySearchResults
    search = TavilySearchResults(max_results=3)
    results = search.invoke(query)
    return str(results)

@tool
def calculator(expression: str) -> str:
    """Evaluate a mathematical expression safely.
    
    Args:
        expression: Math expression like '2 + 2 * 10'
    """
    try:
        # Use ast.literal_eval or numexpr for safety in production
        result = eval(expression, {"__builtins__": {}}, {})
        return f"Result: {result}"
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Error: {e}"

# ── Method 2: Pydantic schema (complex inputs) ─────────────
class DatabaseQueryInput(BaseModel):
    table: str = Field(description="Table name to query")
    filters: dict = Field(default={}, description="WHERE clause filters")
    limit: int = Field(default=10, description="Max rows to return")

def query_database(table: str, filters: dict, limit: int) -> str:
    """Query the internal database."""
    # Safe parameterized query implementation
    return f"Results from {table}: ..."

db_tool = StructuredTool.from_function(
    func=query_database,
    name="query_database",
    description="Query internal company database tables",
    args_schema=DatabaseQueryInput,
)

# ── Method 3: Retriever as tool ────────────────────────────
from langchain.tools.retriever import create_retriever_tool
retriever_tool = create_retriever_tool(
    retriever=retriever,
    name="search_knowledge_base",
    description="Search company knowledge base for internal policies, documentation, and procedures. Use for any question about internal processes.",
)

# ── Bind tools to LLM ─────────────────────────────────────
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI
llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o", temperature=0)
tools = [search_web, calculator, db_tool, retriever_tool]
llm_with_tools = llm.bind_tools(tools)
⚠️
Tool Safety Rules1) Never use bare eval() in production tools. 2) Always validate and sanitize tool inputs. 3) Use least-privilege DB access. 4) Add rate limiting to external API tools. 5) Log all tool invocations.
🔁 ToolNode & the Agent Loop Intermediate
Wire up the ReAct agent loop: LLM decides → ToolNode executes → LLM observes → repeat.
intermediatecodetoolsagentlanggraph
▼
python
from langgraph.prebuilt import ToolNode, tools_condition
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, SystemMessage
from typing import TypedDict, Annotated, List, Sequence
from langchain_core.messages import BaseMessage

class AgentState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[Sequence[BaseMessage], add_messages]

# Define tools
tools = [search_web, calculator, retriever_tool]

# LLM with tools bound
llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o", temperature=0)
llm_with_tools = llm.bind_tools(tools)

SYSTEM_PROMPT = """You are a helpful research assistant.
Use search_web for current information.
Use search_knowledge_base for company-internal documents.
Use calculator for any math.
Always cite your sources."""

# ── Agent node: calls LLM ──────────────────────────────────
def agent_node(state: AgentState) -> dict:
    messages = [SystemMessage(content=SYSTEM_PROMPT)] + list(state["messages"])
    response = llm_with_tools.invoke(messages)
    return {"messages": [response]}

# ── ToolNode: executes tools automatically ─────────────────
tool_node = ToolNode(tools)  # Handles tool execution + error catching

# ── Build the ReAct loop ───────────────────────────────────
builder = StateGraph(AgentState)
builder.add_node("agent", agent_node)
builder.add_node("tools", tool_node)

builder.add_edge(START, "agent")

# tools_condition: returns "tools" if LLM wants to call a tool, else END
builder.add_conditional_edges(
    "agent",
    tools_condition,  # Built-in: checks for tool_calls in last message
    {"tools": "tools", END: END}
)

# After tool execution, always return to agent
builder.add_edge("tools", "agent")

# Compile with memory
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver
graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=MemorySaver())

# Run
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "sess-001"}}
result = graph.invoke(
    {"messages": [HumanMessage("What's the latest news on AI? Also, what's 15% of 2500?")]},
    config=config
)
print(result["messages"][-1].content)
🔄
The ReAct LoopAgent → (decides to use tool) → ToolNode → (returns ToolMessage) → Agent → (has results, answers) → END. The loop continues until the LLM decides it has enough information and doesn't request any more tools.
🌐 REST API & External Tool Integration
🌐 Calling REST APIs from LangChain Tools Intermediate
Turn any REST API into an agent-callable tool — with auth, retries, and typed inputs via Pydantic.
intermediatecodetoolsagent
▼
💡
PatternWrap any HTTP endpoint in a @tool function. The agent reads the docstring to decide when and how to call it. Write clear, descriptive docstrings with parameter descriptions.
Simple REST API Tools
python
import os, requests
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

# -- 1. Simple GET tool -----------------------------------------------
@tool
def get_weather(city: str) -> str:
    """Get current weather for a city. Use when the user asks about weather."""
    url = f"https://wttr.in/{city}?format=3"
    response = requests.get(url, timeout=10)
    response.raise_for_status()
    return response.text

# -- 2. Typed inputs with Pydantic ------------------------------------
class SearchInput(BaseModel):
    query: str = Field(description="Search query string")
    max_results: int = Field(default=5, description="Number of results (1-10)")

@tool(args_schema=SearchInput)
def web_search(query: str, max_results: int = 5) -> str:
    """Search the web for current information. Returns top results as text."""
    url = "https://api.duckduckgo.com/"
    params = {"q": query, "format": "json", "no_html": 1}
    data = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=10).json()
    results = []
    if data.get("AbstractText"):
        results.append(f"Summary: {data['AbstractText']}")
    for topic in data.get("RelatedTopics", [])[:max_results]:
        if "Text" in topic:
            results.append(f"- {topic['Text']}")
    return "\n".join(results) if results else "No results found."

# -- 3. POST with JSON body -------------------------------------------
@tool
def create_task(title: str, description: str, priority: str = "medium") -> str:
    """Create a task in the project management system."""
    resp = requests.post(
        "https://api.example.com/tasks",
        json={"title": title, "description": description, "priority": priority},
        headers={
            "Authorization": f"Bearer {os.getenv('API_TOKEN')}",
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
        },
        timeout=15,
    )
    resp.raise_for_status()
    task = resp.json()
    return f"Task created: ID={task['id']}, URL={task.get('url', 'N/A')}"
Robust Session with Auto-Retry
python
from requests.adapters import HTTPAdapter
from urllib3.util.retry import Retry
import requests, os

def make_session(retries: int = 3, backoff: float = 0.5) -> requests.Session:
    """Session with automatic retry on 429/5xx errors."""
    session = requests.Session()
    retry = Retry(total=retries, backoff_factor=backoff,
                  status_forcelist=[429, 500, 502, 503, 504])
    adapter = HTTPAdapter(max_retries=retry)
    session.mount("http://", adapter)
    session.mount("https://", adapter)
    return session

_session = make_session()

@tool
def call_api(endpoint: str, method: str = "GET", payload: dict = None) -> str:
    """
    Call an arbitrary REST API endpoint.
    endpoint: Full URL. method: GET, POST, PUT, DELETE. payload: JSON body for POST/PUT.
    """
    try:
        resp = _session.request(
            method=method.upper(), url=endpoint, json=payload, timeout=20,
            headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {os.getenv('API_TOKEN', '')}"},
        )
        resp.raise_for_status()
        try:
            return str(resp.json())
        except Exception:
            return resp.text[:2000]
    except requests.exceptions.Timeout:
        return "Error: Request timed out (20s)."
    except requests.exceptions.HTTPError as e:
        return f"Error: HTTP {e.response.status_code} -- {e.response.text[:200]}"
    except requests.exceptions.ConnectionError:
        return "Error: Could not connect. Check URL and network."
Authentication Patterns
python
import os, requests

# API Key in header (most common)
headers = {"X-API-Key": os.getenv("MY_API_KEY")}

# Bearer token (OAuth2 / JWT)
headers = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {os.getenv('ACCESS_TOKEN')}"}

# Basic auth
auth = requests.auth.HTTPBasicAuth(os.getenv("USER"), os.getenv("PASS"))
resp = requests.get(url, auth=auth)

# OAuth2 client credentials flow
def get_oauth_token() -> str:
    resp = requests.post(
        "https://auth.example.com/oauth/token",
        data={
            "grant_type": "client_credentials",
            "client_id": os.getenv("CLIENT_ID"),
            "client_secret": os.getenv("CLIENT_SECRET"),
        },
    )
    resp.raise_for_status()
    return resp.json()["access_token"]

token = get_oauth_token()
headers = {"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"}
🔒
Always use environment variables for secrets. Never hardcode API keys. Use python-dotenv locally and cloud secrets in production.
⚡ Serving Your LangGraph Agent as a REST API (FastAPI) Advanced
Expose your compiled LangGraph graph as a production REST API with streaming support via Server-Sent Events.
advancedcodeops
▼
bash
pip install fastapi uvicorn langchain-openai langgraph
python
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException
from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
from pydantic import BaseModel
import uvicorn, json
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

app = FastAPI(title="LangGraph Agent API", version="1.0")

from my_agent import build_graph   # your compiled LangGraph graph
graph = build_graph()

class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    message: str
    thread_id: str = "default"

class ChatResponse(BaseModel):
    answer: str
    thread_id: str

# -- Non-streaming endpoint ------------------------------------------
@app.post("/chat", response_model=ChatResponse)
async def chat(req: ChatRequest):
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": req.thread_id}}
    try:
        result = graph.invoke(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=req.message)]}, config=config)
        return ChatResponse(answer=result["messages"][-1].content,
                            thread_id=req.thread_id)
    except Exception as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=str(e))

# -- Server-Sent Events streaming endpoint ---------------------------
@app.post("/chat/stream")
async def chat_stream(req: ChatRequest):
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": req.thread_id}}

    async def event_generator():
        try:
            async for event in graph.astream_events(
                {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=req.message)]},
                config=config, version="v2",
            ):
                if event["event"] == "on_chat_model_stream":
                    chunk = event["data"]["chunk"].content
                    if chunk:
                        yield f"data: {json.dumps({'delta': chunk})}\n\n"
            yield "data: [DONE]\n\n"
        except Exception as e:
            yield f"data: {json.dumps({'error': str(e)})}\n\n"

    return StreamingResponse(
        event_generator(), media_type="text/event-stream",
        headers={"Cache-Control": "no-cache", "X-Accel-Buffering": "no"})

@app.get("/health")
def health(): return {"status": "ok"}

if __name__ == "__main__":
    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=8000)
JavaScript Client (Consuming the Streaming API)
python
// JavaScript client for the SSE streaming endpoint
const response = await fetch('/chat/stream', {
  method: 'POST',
  headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
  body: JSON.stringify({message: 'Hello', thread_id: 'user-123'})
});
const reader = response.body.getReader();
const decoder = new TextDecoder();
while (true) {
  const {done, value} = await reader.read();
  if (done) break;
  const lines = decoder.decode(value).split('\n');
  for (const line of lines) {
    if (!line.startsWith('data:')) continue;
    const text = line.slice(5).trim();
    if (text === '[DONE]') break;
    const data = JSON.parse(text);
    process.stdout.write(data.delta || '');
  }
}
🔗 LangChain Expression Language (LCEL)
🔗 LCEL — Advanced Chain Patterns Intermediate
Master LCEL's powerful composition primitives: parallel execution, branching, passthrough, and more.
intermediatecodelangchain
▼
python
from langchain_core.runnables import (
    RunnablePassthrough,    # Pass input unchanged to next step
    RunnableParallel,       # Run multiple chains at once (parallel)
    RunnableLambda,         # Wrap any function as a Runnable
    RunnableBranch,         # Route to different chains
)
from operator import itemgetter

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.2", temperature=0)

# ── 1. RunnablePassthrough — preserve original input ──────
# Input: {"question": "...", "context": "..."}
# After | RunnablePassthrough() — same dict flows through

# Common RAG pattern:
chain = (
    {"context": retriever | format_docs, "question": RunnablePassthrough()}
    | prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()
)

# ── 2. RunnableParallel — concurrent execution ─────────────
# Runs both branches simultaneously, merges results
parallel_chain = RunnableParallel(
    summary=ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Summarize: {text}") | llm | StrOutputParser(),
    keywords=ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Extract 5 keywords from: {text}") | llm | StrOutputParser(),
    sentiment=ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("What is the sentiment of: {text}? (positive/negative/neutral)") | llm | StrOutputParser(),
)
# Returns: {"summary": "...", "keywords": "...", "sentiment": "..."}
result = parallel_chain.invoke({"text": "LangGraph is amazing for building complex AI agents!"})

# ── 3. RunnableLambda — function as Runnable ──────────────
def preprocess(input_dict: dict) -> dict:
    return {"text": input_dict["text"].strip().lower()}

chain = RunnableLambda(preprocess) | parallel_chain

# ── 4. itemgetter — extract specific keys ─────────────────
# Input dict has many keys, extract only "question"
extract_question = itemgetter("question")

chain = (
    ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Answer: {question}")
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)
# chain.invoke({"question": "Hi", "user_id": "123"}) — works, ignores user_id

# ── 5. RunnableBranch — conditional routing ────────────────
is_technical = lambda x: any(kw in x["question"].lower()
                              for kw in ["code", "error", "function", "bug"])

branch = RunnableBranch(
    (is_technical,
     ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("You are a code expert. {question}") | llm),
    # Default (no condition matches):
    ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("You are a general assistant. {question}") | llm,
)
answer = (branch | StrOutputParser()).invoke({"question": "How do I fix this Python error?"})

# ── 6. .assign() — add to existing dict ───────────────────
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnablePassthrough

chain = (
    RunnablePassthrough.assign(
        docs=lambda x: retriever.invoke(x["question"]),
        context=lambda x: format_docs(retriever.invoke(x["question"])),
    )
    | prompt
    | llm
    | StrOutputParser()
)
# Input: {"question": "..."}  →  {"question": "...", "docs": [...], "context": "..."}
🧠 LangChain Memory Patterns
🧠 Conversation Memory Patterns Intermediate
Implement multi-turn conversation memory: full history, sliding window, and summarized memory.
intermediatecodelangchainmemory
▼
💡
Modern approachIn LangGraph, memory is handled by the state's messages field + checkpointers. The patterns below are for standalone LCEL chains and chatbots outside LangGraph.
python
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate, MessagesPlaceholder
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, AIMessage
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.2", temperature=0.3)

# ── Pattern 1: Manual history (simplest, full control) ────
history_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a helpful assistant. Be concise."),
    MessagesPlaceholder("history"),
    ("human", "{question}"),
])

chain = history_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()

chat_history = []

def chat(question: str) -> str:
    response = chain.invoke({
        "history": chat_history,
        "question": question,
    })
    # Append to history for next turn
    chat_history.append(HumanMessage(content=question))
    chat_history.append(AIMessage(content=response))
    return response

print(chat("My name is Alice and I love Python."))
print(chat("What's my name?"))   # "Your name is Alice."
print(chat("What do I love?"))   # "You love Python."

# ── Pattern 2: Sliding window (limit history length) ──────
MAX_HISTORY = 6  # Keep last 6 messages (3 exchanges)

def chat_windowed(question: str) -> str:
    response = chain.invoke({
        "history": chat_history[-MAX_HISTORY:],  # Trim!
        "question": question,
    })
    chat_history.append(HumanMessage(content=question))
    chat_history.append(AIMessage(content=response))
    return response

# ── Pattern 3: Summarized memory (handles long conversations)
SUMMARY_PROMPT = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Summarize the following conversation, keeping key facts:
{conversation}
Summary:""")

summary_chain = SUMMARY_PROMPT | llm | StrOutputParser()

def summarize_history(history: list) -> str:
    if len(history) < 4:
        return ""
    convo = "\n".join(
        f"{'User' if isinstance(m, HumanMessage) else 'AI'}: {m.content}"
        for m in history
    )
    return summary_chain.invoke({"conversation": convo})

# Prompt with summary + recent messages
summary_aware_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a helpful assistant.\n{summary}"),
    MessagesPlaceholder("recent_history"),
    ("human", "{question}"),
])

summary = ""
recent = []

def chat_summarized(question: str) -> str:
    global summary
    response = (summary_aware_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()).invoke({
        "summary": f"Conversation so far: {summary}" if summary else "",
        "recent_history": recent[-4:],
        "question": question,
    })
    recent.append(HumanMessage(content=question))
    recent.append(AIMessage(content=response))
    # Summarize after every 10 messages
    if len(recent) >= 10:
        summary = summarize_history(recent)
        recent.clear()
    return response
💬 Multi-turn Chatbot: Ollama + LangGraph Memory Intermediate
Build a persistent multi-session chatbot using local Ollama and LangGraph's checkpointer for memory.
intermediatecodeollamalangchainlanggraphmemorylocal
▼
python
"""
Production-ready multi-turn chatbot:
- Local Ollama LLM (free, private)
- LangGraph for graph-based flow
- Checkpointer for persistent memory
- Multiple concurrent sessions via thread_id
"""

from typing import Annotated, Sequence, TypedDict
from langchain_core.messages import BaseMessage, SystemMessage, HumanMessage, trim_messages
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

# ── State ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
class ChatState(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[Sequence[BaseMessage], add_messages]

# ── LLM ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
llm = ChatOllama(
    model="llama3.1:8b",
    temperature=0.7,
    num_ctx=8192,
)

SYSTEM_PROMPT = """You are a helpful, friendly assistant.
You remember everything from our conversation.
Be concise but thorough."""

# Message trimmer — prevent context overflow
trimmer = trim_messages(
    max_tokens=4000,
    strategy="last",       # Keep the most recent messages
    token_counter=llm,
    include_system=True,
    allow_partial=False,
    start_on="human",
)

# ── Node ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
def chat_node(state: ChatState) -> dict:
    messages = [SystemMessage(content=SYSTEM_PROMPT)] + list(state["messages"])
    trimmed = trimmer.invoke(messages)  # Trim to fit context window
    response = llm.invoke(trimmed)
    return {"messages": [response]}

# ── Graph ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
builder = StateGraph(ChatState)
builder.add_node("chat", chat_node)
builder.add_edge(START, "chat")
builder.add_edge("chat", END)

checkpointer = MemorySaver()
graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=checkpointer)

# ── Multi-session usage ───────────────────────────────────
def ask(question: str, session_id: str = "default") -> str:
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": session_id}}
    result = graph.invoke(
        {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=question)]},
        config=config
    )
    return result["messages"][-1].content

# Session 1 — Alice
print(ask("My favourite language is Python.", session_id="alice"))
print(ask("What's my favourite language?", session_id="alice"))  # Remembers Python!

# Session 2 — Bob — completely separate memory
print(ask("I prefer TypeScript.", session_id="bob"))
print(ask("What language do I prefer?", session_id="bob"))       # TypeScript
print(ask("What language do I prefer?", session_id="alice"))     # Still Python!

# ── Interactive REPL ──────────────────────────────────────
if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("🦙 Local Ollama Chatbot (type 'quit' to exit)")
    session = input("Session name: ").strip() or "default"
    while True:
        user_input = input("You: ").strip()
        if user_input.lower() in ("quit", "exit", "q"):
            break
        response = ask(user_input, session_id=session)
        print(f"Bot: {response}\n")
🐛 Ollama Troubleshooting Guide Beginner
Solutions for the most common Ollama + LangChain problems.
beginnerollamalocal
▼
ProblemCauseFix
Connection refused: localhost:11434Ollama server not runningRun ollama serve in a terminal, or restart the Ollama app.
Model not found errorModel not downloadedRun ollama pull <model-name> first.
Response is very slowNo GPU, large model on CPUUse smaller model (llama3.2:3b, phi3:mini). Install Ollama on GPU machine.
Context window errornum_ctx too smallSet num_ctx=4096 or higher in ChatOllama().
Tool calling not workingModel doesn't support toolsSwitch to llama3.1:8b or qwen2.5:7b. Not all models support this.
Out of memory (OOM)Model too large for RAMUse a smaller model. Close other apps. Enable GPU offload if partial GPU.
Garbled/repetitive outputrepeat_penalty neededSet in Modelfile: PARAMETER repeat_penalty 1.1
LangChain timeout errorDefault timeout too shortSet ChatOllama(timeout=120) for slow models.
Embeddings dimension mismatchChanged embedding modelDelete ./chroma_db and re-embed with consistent model.
Quick Diagnostics

# Check Ollama is running
