## tests/unit/test_browser_tools.py
import pytest
from unittest.mock import AsyncMock, MagicMock, patch

class TestBrowserTools:
    @pytest.mark.asyncio
    async def test_navigate_to_calls_goto(self):
        """Verify navigate_to invokes page.goto with the correct URL."""
        mock_page = AsyncMock()
        mock_page.title = AsyncMock(return_value="Example Domain")
        mock_page.goto = AsyncMock()

        with patch("my_app.tools.browser.get_page", return_value=mock_page):
            from my_app.tools.browser import navigate_to   # adjust import
            # result = await navigate_to.ainvoke({"url": "https://example.com"})
            # assert "Example Domain" in result
            # mock_page.goto.assert_called_once_with("https://example.com",
            #                                         wait_until="domcontentloaded", timeout=30_000)

    @pytest.mark.asyncio
    async def test_get_page_text_truncates(self):
        """Verify page text is truncated to avoid token overflow."""
        mock_page = AsyncMock()
        mock_page.inner_text = AsyncMock(return_value="word " * 5000)   # 25000 chars

        # The tool should truncate to 6000 chars
        text = await mock_page.inner_text("body")
        truncated = text[:6000]
        assert len(truncated) <= 6000

    @pytest.mark.asyncio
    async def test_scrape_webpage_strips_nav(self):
        """Test HTML parsing removes nav/footer/scripts."""
        from bs4 import BeautifulSoup

        html = """<html>
        <nav>Nav links</nav>
        <main>Main content here</main>
        <footer>Footer text</footer>
        <script>var x = 1;</script>
        </html>"""

        soup = BeautifulSoup(html, "html.parser")
        for tag in soup(["script", "nav", "footer"]):
            tag.decompose()
        text = soup.get_text(separator="\n", strip=True)
        assert "Main content" in text
        assert "Nav links" not in text
        assert "Footer text" not in text
        assert "var x = 1" not in text
🚀 CI/CD Integration & pytest Configuration Advanced
Configure pytest for CI, separate fast unit tests from slow integration tests, and run the right tests at the right time.
advancedcodetestingops
▼
pytest.ini / pyproject.toml
python
# pyproject.toml
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
asyncio_mode = "auto"            # auto-detect async tests (needs pytest-asyncio)
testpaths = ["tests"]
python_files = ["test_*.py", "*_test.py"]
python_classes = ["Test*"]
python_functions = ["test_*"]

# Custom markers to separate test tiers
markers = [
    "unit: fast unit tests with mocked LLMs (no API key required)",
    "integration: slower tests requiring Ollama or a local service",
    "e2e: end-to-end tests against real LLMs (requires API keys, skip in CI)",
    "slow: any test taking longer than 10 seconds",
]

# Default: run only unit tests in CI (skip slow/integration/e2e)
addopts = "-m 'unit' --tb=short -q"
conftest.py — Shared Fixtures
python
## tests/conftest.py
import pytest
from langchain_core.language_models import FakeListChatModel
from langchain_community.embeddings import FakeEmbeddings
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS
from langchain_core.documents import Document

@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def fake_llm():
    """Session-scoped fake LLM — shared across all tests for speed."""
    return FakeListChatModel(responses=["Test response " + str(i) for i in range(100)])

@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def fake_embeddings():
    return FakeEmbeddings(size=1536)

@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def sample_docs():
    return [
        Document(page_content="LangGraph is a framework for building stateful agents.",
                 metadata={"source": "langgraph.txt"}),
        Document(page_content="Ollama lets you run open-source LLMs locally.",
                 metadata={"source": "ollama.txt"}),
        Document(page_content="RAG combines retrieval with LLM generation.",
                 metadata={"source": "rag.txt"}),
    ]

@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def vectorstore(sample_docs, fake_embeddings):
    return FAISS.from_documents(sample_docs, fake_embeddings)

@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def retriever(vectorstore):
    return vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 2})

# Mark slow tests to skip unless explicitly run
def pytest_configure(config):
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "unit: fast unit tests")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "integration: requires Ollama")
    config.addinivalue_line("markers", "e2e: requires real LLM API key")
GitHub Actions CI Workflow
bash
# .github/workflows/test.yml
name: Unit Tests
on: [push, pull_request]

jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
        with: {python-version: "3.11"}
      - run: pip install pytest pytest-asyncio langchain-core langchain-community faiss-cpu
      - run: pytest -m "unit" --tb=short -q   # Only unit tests, no API keys needed

  # Run integration tests nightly (optional — needs Ollama self-hosted runner)
  integration:
    runs-on: self-hosted   # Your Ollama-enabled runner
    if: github.event_name == 'schedule'
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - run: pip install -r requirements.txt
      - run: pytest -m "integration" --tb=short
Parametrize Tests for Multiple Models
python
import pytest
from langchain_core.language_models import FakeListChatModel
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser

@pytest.mark.parametrize("response,expected_contains", [
    ("Paris is the capital.", "Paris"),
    ("The answer is 42.", "42"),
    ("I don't know.", "don't know"),
])
def test_chain_with_various_llm_responses(response, expected_contains):
    """Run the same test with different simulated LLM responses."""
    llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=[response])
    chain = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("Q: {q}") | llm | StrOutputParser()
    result = chain.invoke({"q": "test"})
    assert expected_contains in result

@pytest.mark.parametrize("k", [1, 2, 5])
def test_retriever_respects_k(vectorstore, k):
    retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": k})
    docs = retriever.invoke("test query")
    assert len(docs) <= k
🐛 Debugging Agentic AI Workflows
📊 LangGraph Built-in Debug Tools Intermediate
Visualise your graph structure, inspect every state transition, and stream debug events — all with zero extra dependencies.
intermediatedebugginglanggraph
▼
Visualise graph structure before running
python
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, MessagesState

builder = StateGraph(MessagesState)
# ... add nodes and edges ...
graph = builder.compile()

# ASCII diagram in terminal — fastest way to spot wrong edges
print(graph.get_graph().draw_ascii())

# Mermaid diagram — paste at mermaid.live for a rendered flowchart
print(graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid())

# PNG file (requires pygraphviz: pip install pygraphviz)
graph.get_graph().draw_png("graph_debug.png")

# For subgraphs — recurse into nested graphs
print(graph.get_graph(xray=True).draw_ascii())
Stream every event for step-by-step inspection
python
import json

# stream_mode="debug" emits raw internal events — every node entry/exit + state
for event in graph.stream(
    {"messages": [("user", "What is 2 + 2?")]},
    stream_mode="debug",
):
    print(json.dumps(event, indent=2, default=str))
    # Output includes: type (task/task_result/checkpoint), step, payload, timestamp

# stream_mode="values" — full state snapshot after every node
for state in graph.stream(
    {"messages": [("user", "test")]},
    stream_mode="values",
):
    print("STATE AFTER NODE:", state)

# stream_mode="updates" — only the delta each node produced
for node_name, update in graph.stream(
    {"messages": [("user", "test")]},
    stream_mode="updates",
):
    print(f"  {node_name} → {update}")
Inspect state at any checkpoint (time-travel)
python
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

memory = MemorySaver()
graph  = builder.compile(checkpointer=memory)
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "debug-001"}}

# Run a few turns
graph.invoke({"messages": [("user", "First message")]}, config=config)
graph.invoke({"messages": [("user", "Second message")]}, config=config)

# List all saved checkpoints for this thread
history = list(graph.get_state_history(config))
print(f"{len(history)} checkpoints saved")

for i, snapshot in enumerate(history):
    print(f"\n── Checkpoint {i} ────────────────────")
    print("  Step:     ", snapshot.metadata.get("step"))
    print("  Node:     ", snapshot.metadata.get("source"))
    print("  Msg count:", len(snapshot.values.get("messages", [])))
    print("  Next:     ", snapshot.next)

# Replay from a specific checkpoint (time-travel)
past_config = history[1].config   # second-most-recent
past_state  = graph.get_state(past_config)
print("\nReplaying from checkpoint:", past_state.values["messages"][-1].content)

# Fork and replay from that point with a different input
graph.update_state(past_config, {"messages": [("user", "Different question")]})
result = graph.invoke(None, config=past_config)
Enable verbose LangChain tracing globally
python
import langchain
langchain.debug   = True   # prints every LLM input/output
langchain.verbose = True   # prints every chain step summary

# Turn off after debugging
langchain.debug   = False
langchain.verbose = False

⛹️ Breakpoint Debugging — Pause & Inspect Mid-Graph Intermediate
LangGraph's interrupt mechanism doubles as a debugger — pause execution at any node, inspect state, modify it, then resume. No monkey-patching required.
intermediatedebugginglanggraph
▼
python
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, MessagesState
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama

llm      = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1")
memory   = MemorySaver()

def node_a(state): return {"messages": [llm.invoke(state["messages"])]}
def node_b(state): return {"messages": [llm.invoke(state["messages"])]}
def node_c(state): return {"messages": [llm.invoke(state["messages"])]}

builder = StateGraph(MessagesState)
builder.add_node("A", node_a)
builder.add_node("B", node_b)
builder.add_node("C", node_c)
builder.set_entry_point("A")
builder.add_edge("A", "B")
builder.add_edge("B", "C")
builder.set_finish_point("C")

# ── Compile with interrupt BEFORE node B ─────────────────────────────
graph  = builder.compile(checkpointer=memory, interrupt_before=["B"])
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "debug-break-01"}}

# ── Run until the breakpoint ──────────────────────────────────────────
result = graph.invoke(
    {"messages": [("user", "Tell me a joke")]},
    config=config,
)
print("Paused. Next node:", graph.get_state(config).next)

# ── Inspect state at the breakpoint ──────────────────────────────────
state = graph.get_state(config)
print("Messages so far:")
for m in state.values["messages"]:
    print(f"  [{m.type}] {m.content[:80]}")

# ── Optionally modify state before resuming ───────────────────────────
# e.g. inject a system correction
graph.update_state(
    config,
    {"messages": [("system", "Be more concise in your response.")]},
)

# ── Resume from breakpoint ────────────────────────────────────────────
final = graph.invoke(None, config=config)
print("Final:", final["messages"][-1].content)
💡
Multiple breakpoints
interrupt_before=["B", "C"] — pause before both nodes, resume twice.
interrupt_after=["A"] — pause after node A completes (inspect its output before the next node runs).
🧪 Deterministic Unit Testing of Graph Nodes Advanced
Test each node in isolation with FakeListChatModel — no Ollama, no API key, no network — deterministic assertions every time.
advancedtestingdebugging
▼
bash
pip install pytest pytest-asyncio langchain-core
python
# tests/test_graph_nodes.py
import pytest
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage, AIMessage
from langchain_core.language_models.fake import FakeListChatModel
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, MessagesState
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

# ── Build graph with an injected LLM (dependency injection) ──────────
def build_graph(llm):
    def chat_node(state):
        return {"messages": [llm.invoke(state["messages"])]}

    def should_continue(state):
        last = state["messages"][-1]
        return "tools" if "SEARCH:" in last.content else "end"

    builder = StateGraph(MessagesState)
    builder.add_node("chat", chat_node)
    builder.set_entry_point("chat")
    builder.add_conditional_edges("chat", should_continue, {"tools": "chat", "end": "__end__"})
    return builder.compile(checkpointer=MemorySaver())

# ── Tests ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
class TestChatNode:
    def test_simple_response(self):
        fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=["Hello, I am a helpful assistant."])
        graph  = build_graph(fake_llm)
        result = graph.invoke(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="Hi")]},
            config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "t1"}},
        )
        assert result["messages"][-1].content == "Hello, I am a helpful assistant."

    def test_routes_to_tools_on_search_keyword(self):
        """If LLM output contains 'SEARCH:' the graph should loop."""
        fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=[
            "SEARCH: latest LangGraph release",   # first call → trigger tool route
            "The latest release is v0.2.",         # second call → final answer
        ])
        graph  = build_graph(fake_llm)
        result = graph.invoke(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="What is the latest LangGraph?")]},
            config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "t2"}},
        )
        msgs = result["messages"]
        assert any("SEARCH:" in m.content for m in msgs), "Should have triggered search"
        assert msgs[-1].content == "The latest release is v0.2."
        assert len(msgs) >= 3, "Should have looped through chat twice"

    def test_state_accumulates_messages(self):
        fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=["Response 1", "Response 2"])
        graph    = build_graph(fake_llm)
        config   = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "t3"}}
        graph.invoke({"messages": [HumanMessage(content="Turn 1")]}, config=config)
        result = graph.invoke({"messages": [HumanMessage(content="Turn 2")]}, config=config)
        assert len(result["messages"]) == 4   # 2 human + 2 AI

@pytest.mark.asyncio
class TestAsyncNodes:
    async def test_async_invoke(self):
        fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=["Async response"])
        graph    = build_graph(fake_llm)
        result   = await graph.ainvoke(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="async test")]},
            config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "async-t1"}},
        )
        assert result["messages"][-1].content == "Async response"
bash
pytest tests/test_graph_nodes.py -v
# or run async only:
pytest tests/test_graph_nodes.py -v -k "Async"
🎗 Mocking Tools & External Calls in Tests Advanced
Isolate tool calls with unittest.mock — test full agent loops including tool execution without hitting real APIs or databases.
advancedtestingdebugging
▼
python
# tests/test_tool_mocking.py
from unittest.mock import patch, MagicMock
import pytest
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langchain_core.language_models.fake import FakeListChatModel
from langchain_core.messages import AIMessage, ToolCall
from langgraph.prebuilt import create_react_agent
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

# ── Real tools (would normally call external APIs) ────────────────────
@tool
def search_web(query: str) -> str:
    """Search the web for information."""
    import httpx
    return httpx.get(f"https://api.search.com?q={query}").text   # real call

@tool
def get_stock_price(ticker: str) -> str:
    """Get current stock price."""
    import httpx
    return httpx.get(f"https://api.stocks.com/{ticker}").text     # real call

# ── Test with mocked tool implementations ─────────────────────────────
class TestAgentWithMockedTools:

    @patch("__main__.search_web", return_value="LangGraph 0.2.50 was released today.")
    def test_search_tool_called(self, mock_search):
        # FakeListChatModel that emits a tool call then a final answer
        fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=[
            AIMessage(
                content="",
                tool_calls=[ToolCall(name="search_web", args={"query": "LangGraph latest release"}, id="tc1")]
            ),
            "LangGraph 0.2.50 was released today.",
        ])
        agent  = create_react_agent(fake_llm, [search_web], checkpointer=MemorySaver())
        result = agent.invoke(
            {"messages": [("user", "What is the latest LangGraph release?")]},
            config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "mock-t1"}},
        )
        mock_search.assert_called_once_with(query="LangGraph latest release")
        assert "0.2.50" in result["messages"][-1].content

    def test_tool_error_handled_gracefully(self):
        """Agent should recover when a tool raises an exception."""
        with patch("__main__.get_stock_price", side_effect=ConnectionError("API down")):
            fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=[
                AIMessage(content="", tool_calls=[
                    ToolCall(name="get_stock_price", args={"ticker": "AAPL"}, id="tc2")
                ]),
                "I was unable to retrieve the stock price due to an error.",
            ])
            agent  = create_react_agent(fake_llm, [get_stock_price], checkpointer=MemorySaver())
            result = agent.invoke(
                {"messages": [("user", "What is Apple's stock price?")]},
                config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "mock-t2"}},
            )
            # Agent should still produce a final message, not crash
            assert len(result["messages"]) > 0

    def test_multiple_tool_calls_in_sequence(self):
        """Test agents that call multiple tools in one turn."""
        search_mock = MagicMock(return_value="LangChain docs found.")
        stock_mock  = MagicMock(return_value="AAPL: $185.00")

        with patch("__main__.search_web", search_mock), \
             patch("__main__.get_stock_price", stock_mock):

            fake_llm = FakeListChatModel(responses=[
                AIMessage(content="", tool_calls=[
                    ToolCall(name="search_web",    args={"query": "langchain"}, id="tc3"),
                    ToolCall(name="get_stock_price",args={"ticker": "AAPL"},   id="tc4"),
                ]),
                "Here is the combined result.",
            ])
            agent  = create_react_agent(fake_llm, [search_web, get_stock_price], checkpointer=MemorySaver())
            result = agent.invoke(
                {"messages": [("user", "Tell me about LangChain and Apple stock")]},
                config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "mock-t3"}},
            )
            search_mock.assert_called_once()
            stock_mock.assert_called_once()
🔎 RAG Pipeline Debugging Advanced
Diagnose why RAG gives wrong or empty answers — inspect retrieved chunks, relevance scores, and prompt construction at each stage.
advanceddebuggingrag
▼
Step 1 — Inspect what the retriever actually returns
python
from langchain_ollama import OllamaEmbeddings
from langchain_community.vectorstores import FAISS

embeddings  = OllamaEmbeddings(model="nomic-embed-text")
vectorstore = FAISS.load_local("my_index", embeddings, allow_dangerous_deserialization=True)
retriever   = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 4})

query = "What is the refund policy?"

# ── Raw retrieval with scores ─────────────────────────────────────────
docs_with_scores = vectorstore.similarity_search_with_score(query, k=6)
print(f"\nRetrieved {len(docs_with_scores)} chunks for: '{query}'\n")
for i, (doc, score) in enumerate(docs_with_scores):
    print(f"── Chunk {i+1} (score={score:.4f}) ─────")
    print(f"   Source : {doc.metadata.get('source','?')}")
    print(f"   Content: {doc.page_content[:200]}")
    print()

# ── Rule of thumb: score interpretation (L2 distance for FAISS) ───────
# < 0.3  → very relevant
# 0.3–0.7 → somewhat relevant
# > 0.7  → probably not useful — consider increasing k or re-chunking
Step 2 — Log the full prompt sent to the LLM
python
from langchain_core.callbacks.base import BaseCallbackHandler

class PromptLogger(BaseCallbackHandler):
    """Logs every prompt sent to the LLM — attach to any chain."""
    def on_llm_start(self, serialized, prompts, **kwargs):
        print("\n══ PROMPT SENT TO LLM ══════════════════════")
        for i, p in enumerate(prompts):
            print(f"[Prompt {i}]\n{p}")
        print("════════════════════════════════════════════\n")

    def on_llm_end(self, response, **kwargs):
        print("\n══ LLM RESPONSE ════════════════════════════")
        for gen in response.generations:
            for g in gen:
                print(g.text[:500])
        print("════════════════════════════════════════════\n")

# Attach to a chain
chain = prompt | llm.with_config(callbacks=[PromptLogger()]) | StrOutputParser()
chain.invoke({"question": query, "context": "..."})
Step 3 — Common RAG failure modes & fixes
SymptomLikely causeFix
Answer says "I don't know" but doc existsChunk too large — key info split across chunksReduce chunk_size, increase chunk_overlap
Retrieves wrong chunksEmbedding model mismatch (indexed with one, querying with another)Always use the same embedding model for index & query
Hallucinated answerRetrieved chunks are irrelevant (high score)Add score threshold: retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_type="similarity_score_threshold", search_kwargs={"score_threshold": 0.5})
Works for short docs, fails for longk=4 not enough for dense documentsIncrease k; use MMR retrieval to diversify results
Slow first queryFAISS index loadingLoad at startup; cache the retriever in app state
Automated retrieval quality check
python
def audit_retriever(retriever, qa_pairs: list[dict]) -> None:
    """Quick sanity check: are expected answers present in retrieved chunks?"""
    print(f"\nAuditing {len(qa_pairs)} Q&A pairs...\n")
    for pair in qa_pairs:
        docs    = retriever.invoke(pair["question"])
        content = " ".join(d.page_content for d in docs).lower()
        found   = pair["expected_keyword"].lower() in content
        status  = "✓" if found else "✗ MISS"
        print(f"  {status}  Q: {pair['question'][:60]}")
        if not found:
            print(f"        Expected keyword '{pair['expected_keyword']}' NOT in retrieved chunks")

audit_retriever(retriever, [
    {"question": "What is the refund policy?",  "expected_keyword": "refund"},
    {"question": "How do I reset my password?", "expected_keyword": "password"},
    {"question": "What are the shipping costs?","expected_keyword": "shipping"},
])
🐞 Common Agentic Bugs & How to Find Them Advanced
The most frequent bugs in LangGraph agents with reproducible debugging recipes for each — infinite loops, wrong routing, state corruption, and tool failures.
advanceddebugginglanggraph
▼
Bug 1 — Infinite loop (agent never finishes)
python
# Symptom: graph runs forever or hits RecursionError
# Cause:   conditional edge always returns the loop path

# ── Debug: add recursion limit + trace the routing function ──────────
graph = builder.compile()
try:
    result = graph.invoke(
        {"messages": [("user", "test")]},
        config={"recursion_limit": 10},   # ← raises GraphRecursionError after 10 steps
    )
except Exception as e:
    print(f"Stopped at recursion: {e}")

# ── Add logging to your routing function ─────────────────────────────
def should_continue(state) -> str:
    last_msg = state["messages"][-1]
    decision = "continue" if last_msg.tool_calls else "end"
    print(f"[ROUTER] tool_calls={bool(last_msg.tool_calls)} → {decision}")
    return decision
Bug 2 — State field not updated (reducer issue)
python
from typing import TypedDict, Annotated
from operator import add

# ── Wrong: plain list overwrites on every update ──────────────────────
class BadState(TypedDict):
    results: list[str]        # ← each node REPLACES the list

# ── Right: use Annotated + add to accumulate ──────────────────────────
class GoodState(TypedDict):
    results: Annotated[list[str], add]   # ← each node APPENDS to the list

# ── Debug: print state before and after each node ─────────────────────
def debug_node(state):
    print(f"  BEFORE: results={state.get('results')}")
    new_result = "some output"
    print(f"  RETURNING: results=['{new_result}']")
    return {"results": [new_result]}
Bug 3 — Tool call argument mismatch
python
from langchain_core.tools import tool

@tool
def search(query: str, max_results: int = 5) -> str:
    """Search for information. Args: query (str), max_results (int, default 5)."""
    return f"Results for: {query}"

# ── Debug: print every tool invocation ───────────────────────────────
from langchain_core.callbacks.base import BaseCallbackHandler

class ToolDebugHandler(BaseCallbackHandler):
    def on_tool_start(self, serialized, input_str, **kwargs):
        print(f"\n[TOOL START] {serialized.get('name')} | input: {input_str}")

    def on_tool_end(self, output, **kwargs):
        print(f"[TOOL END]   output: {str(output)[:200]}")

    def on_tool_error(self, error, **kwargs):
        print(f"[TOOL ERROR] {error}")

# Attach to agent
from langgraph.prebuilt import create_react_agent
agent = create_react_agent(llm, [search])
agent.invoke(
    {"messages": [("user", "search for python tutorials")]},
    config={"callbacks": [ToolDebugHandler()], "configurable": {"thread_id": "t1"}},
)
Bug 4 — Memory not persisting across turns
python
from langgraph.checkpoint.memory import MemorySaver

# ── Common mistake: creating a new MemorySaver each invocation ────────
# WRONG — new saver = empty memory every time
def handle_request(msg: str):
    saver  = MemorySaver()          # ← creates fresh saver with no history!
    graph  = builder.compile(checkpointer=saver)
    return graph.invoke(...)

# RIGHT — create saver once at startup, share across requests
saver  = MemorySaver()             # ← created once
graph  = builder.compile(checkpointer=saver)

def handle_request(msg: str, thread_id: str):
    return graph.invoke(
        {"messages": [("user", msg)]},
        config={"configurable": {"thread_id": thread_id}},  # same ID = same memory
    )

# ── Debug: verify memory is working ──────────────────────────────────
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "mem-test"}}
graph.invoke({"messages": [("user", "My name is Alice")]}, config=config)
result = graph.invoke({"messages": [("user", "What is my name?")]}, config=config)
print(result["messages"][-1].content)   # should say "Alice"
Quick debugging checklist
CheckCommand / fix
Graph structure correct?print(graph.get_graph().draw_ascii())
State updating as expected?stream_mode="values" — print state after each node
Routing function choosing right path?Add print() to every conditional edge function
LLM receiving correct prompt?Attach PromptLogger callback; or enable langchain.debug=True
Tool being called with right args?Attach ToolDebugHandler callback
Memory persisting?Call graph twice on same thread, check last message references first
Graph hanging / infinite loop?Set recursion_limit=10 in config
RAG wrong answer?Print vectorstore.similarity_search_with_score(query, k=6)

🔬 Visualize & Debug LangGraph Graphs Beginner
See your graph structure, trace execution, and catch issues before they hit production.
beginnercodelanggraphops

python
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph

# ── Visualize graph structure ─────────────────────────────

# Option 1: Mermaid diagram (paste at https://mermaid.live)
mermaid_code = graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid()

# Output:
# graph TD
#   __start__ --> retrieve

#   generate --> END

# Option 3: ASCII art (no dependencies)

# Option 4: LangGraph Studio (GUI — best for development)
# langgraph dev --port 8000
# Then open http://127.0.0.1:8000 in your browser
# Full interactive graph visualization + state inspection

# ── Trace execution node by node ─────────────────────────
def trace_graph(graph, inputs, config):
    """Print which node runs and what state looks like after each step."""
    print("\n🔍 Graph Execution Trace:")
    print(f"Initial input: {inputs}\n")

    for event in graph.stream(inputs, config, stream_mode="updates"):
        for node_name, state_update in event.items():
            print(f"{'─'*50}")
            print(f"  Node: {node_name}")
            for key, val in state_update.items():
                if isinstance(val, list) and len(val) > 0:
                    # Truncate long lists
                    print(f"  {key}: [{len(val)} items] {str(val[0])[:100]}...")
                else:
                    print(f"  {key}: {str(val)[:100]}")
    print(f"{'─'*50}")
    print("✅ Graph execution complete\n")

trace_graph(graph, {"question": "What is our leave policy?"}, config)

# ── Inspect state at any checkpoint ──────────────────────
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "debug-session"}}

# After running: inspect current state
state = graph.get_state(config)
print(f"Current node(s) queued: {state.next}")
print(f"Messages so far: {len(state.values.get('messages', []))}")
print(f"Step count: {state.metadata.get('step', 0)}")

# Walk through history
print("\n📜 Execution History:")
for i, snapshot in enumerate(graph.get_state_history(config)):
    print(f"  Step {snapshot.metadata.get('step', i)}: {snapshot.next}")

# ── Add logging to every node ─────────────────────────────
import logging
import functools

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

def log_node(func):
    """Decorator: log node entry, exit, and duration."""
    @functools.wraps(func)
    def wrapper(state):
        logger.info(f"→ Node '{func.__name__}' starting")
        import time; t0 = time.time()
        result = func(state)
        ms = (time.time() - t0) * 1000
        logger.info(f"← Node '{func.__name__}' done in {ms:.0f}ms, updated: {list(result.keys())}")
        return result
    return wrapper

@log_node
def my_node(state):
    # ... your logic ...
    return {"answer": "..."}
Common Graph Errors & What They Mean
ErrorMeaningFix
GraphRecursionErrorHit recursion_limit (default 25)Check for infinite loops. Increase limit: config={"recursion_limit": 50}
InvalidUpdateErrorNode returned a key not in state schemaRemove extra keys from node return dict, or add them to the TypedDict.
NodeInterruptGraph paused at interrupt()Expected for HITL. Resume with graph.invoke(Command(resume=...))
State has None valuesNode didn't set a required fieldAdd default values to TypedDict or ensure all nodes populate required fields.
Wrong node orderEdge misconfigurationRun draw_mermaid() and visually verify the flow.

import os

# ── Enable auto-tracing (add to .env) ────────────────────
os.environ["LANGCHAIN_TRACING_V2"] = "true"
os.environ["LANGCHAIN_API_KEY"] = "lsv2_..."
os.environ["LANGCHAIN_PROJECT"] = "production-rag-agent"
os.environ["LANGCHAIN_ENDPOINT"] = "https://api.smith.langchain.com"

# ── Manual tracing with context manager ──────────────────
from langsmith import traceable, trace, Client

@traceable(name="rag_retrieve", run_type="retriever")
def traced_retrieve(query: str) -> list:
    docs = retriever.invoke(query)
    return [d.page_content for d in docs]

# Add custom metadata to traces
@traceable(
    name="answer_question",
    run_type="chain",
    tags=["production", "v2"],
    metadata={"model": "gpt-4o", "temperature": 0}
)
def answer_question(question: str, user_id: str) -> str:
    docs = traced_retrieve(question)
    return llm.invoke(f"Context: {docs}\nQuestion: {question}").content

# ── Evaluate runs in LangSmith ────────────────────────────
from langsmith.evaluation import evaluate as ls_evaluate

def correctness_evaluator(run, example):
    """Custom evaluator that checks answer quality."""
    prompt = f"""
    Is this answer correct and helpful?
    Question: {example.inputs['question']}
    Expected: {example.outputs['answer']}
    Actual: {run.outputs['output']}
    
    Rate 0-1. Only output the number.
    """
    score = float(llm.invoke(prompt).content.strip())
    return {"key": "correctness", "score": score}

client = Client()
results = ls_evaluate(
    lambda x: {"output": answer_question(x["question"], "test-user")},
    data="my-test-dataset",  # Dataset name in LangSmith UI
    evaluators=[correctness_evaluator],
    experiment_prefix="rag-v2",
)

StrategySavingsEffort
Use Ollama for dev/testing100% dev costLow ✅

Semantic Caching (reuse answers for similar questions)
python
from langchain.globals import set_llm_cache
from langchain.cache import InMemoryCache, SQLiteCache

# ── In-memory cache (dev) — exact string match ────────────
set_llm_cache(InMemoryCache())

# ── SQLite cache (persists across restarts) ───────────────
set_llm_cache(SQLiteCache(database_path=".langchain_cache.db"))

# Now ALL llm.invoke() calls are cached automatically!
# Same prompt → instant response, zero API call
llm.invoke("What is RAG?")  # Hits API
llm.invoke("What is RAG?")  # Returns cached result ⚡

# ── Semantic cache (similar questions → same answer) ───────
# pip install langchain-community redis
from langchain_community.cache import RedisSemanticCache
from langchain_openai import OpenAIEmbeddings

semantic_cache = RedisSemanticCache(
    redis_url="redis://localhost:6379",
    embedding=OllamaEmbeddings(model="nomic-embed-text"),  # Free!
    score_threshold=0.15,  # 0=identical, higher=more fuzzy
)
set_llm_cache(semantic_cache)

# "What is RAG?" and "Explain RAG to me" → same cached answer!

# ── Smart Model Routing ───────────────────────────────────
def get_llm_for_task(task_type: str, complexity: str = "low"):
    """Route to cheaper models for simpler tasks."""
    if task_type == "classification" or complexity == "low":
        # Fast, cheap model
        return ChatOllama(model="phi3:mini", temperature=0)
    elif task_type == "reasoning" or complexity == "medium":
        return ChatOllama(model="llama3.1:8b", temperature=0)
    else:
        # Most capable for hard tasks
        return ChatOllama(model="llama3.3:70b", temperature=0)
        # Or cloud: return ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o", temperature=0)

# ── Count tokens before sending ──────────────────────────
import tiktoken

def estimate_cost(text: str, model: str = "gpt-4o") -> dict:
    enc = tiktoken.encoding_for_model(model if model in tiktoken.list_encoding_names() else "gpt-4o")
    tokens = len(enc.encode(text))
    # GPT-4o pricing (as of 2024)
    cost_per_1k = {"gpt-4o": 0.005, "gpt-4o-mini": 0.00015, "gpt-3.5-turbo": 0.002}
    rate = cost_per_1k.get(model, 0.005)
    return {"tokens": tokens, "estimated_cost_usd": tokens / 1000 * rate}

# ── Prompt Compression ────────────────────────────────────
from langchain.retrievers import ContextualCompressionRetriever
from langchain.retrievers.document_compressors import LLMChainExtractor

compressor = LLMChainExtractor.from_llm(ChatOllama(model="phi3:mini"))
compression_retriever = ContextualCompressionRetriever(
    base_compressor=compressor,
    base_retriever=retriever,
    # Extracts only the relevant sentence(s) from each chunk
    # Sends 60-80% fewer tokens to the main LLM
)
🔍 Advanced RAG
🔍 RAG as a LangGraph Workflow Intermediate
Transform a simple RAG chain into a robust LangGraph workflow with quality checks and fallbacks.
intermediatecoderaglanggraph
▼
python
from typing import TypedDict, List, Literal
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from pydantic import BaseModel

class RAGState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    documents: List[str]
    generation: str
    grade: str          # "relevant" | "irrelevant"
    hallucination: str  # "yes" | "no"
    retry_count: int

llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o-mini", temperature=0)

# ── Node 1: Retrieve ──────────────────────────────────────
def retrieve(state: RAGState) -> dict:
    print(f"🔍 Retrieving for: {state['question']}")
    docs = retriever.invoke(state["question"])
    return {"documents": [d.page_content for d in docs]}

# ── Node 2: Grade Documents ───────────────────────────────
class GradeOutput(BaseModel):
    relevant: Literal["yes", "no"]
    
grade_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Is the following document relevant to the question? Answer yes or no.
Question: {question}
Document: {document}
""")
grader = grade_prompt | llm.with_structured_output(GradeOutput)

def grade_documents(state: RAGState) -> dict:
    print("📊 Grading document relevance...")
    filtered = []
    for doc in state["documents"]:
        result = grader.invoke({"question": state["question"], "document": doc})
        if result.relevant == "yes":
            filtered.append(doc)
    return {"documents": filtered, "grade": "relevant" if filtered else "irrelevant"}

# ── Node 3: Generate ──────────────────────────────────────
rag_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Answer the question using ONLY the context below.
Context: {context}
Question: {question}
""")
rag_chain = rag_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()

def generate(state: RAGState) -> dict:
    print("✍️  Generating answer...")
    context = "\n\n".join(state["documents"])
    answer = rag_chain.invoke({"context": context, "question": state["question"]})
    return {"generation": answer}

# ── Node 4: Check Hallucination ───────────────────────────
class HallucinationCheck(BaseModel):
    hallucination: Literal["yes", "no"]

hall_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Is this answer grounded in the given facts? Does it contain information NOT in the facts?
Facts: {documents}
Answer: {generation}
Reply "yes" if hallucinating, "no" if grounded.
""")
hallucination_checker = hall_prompt | llm.with_structured_output(HallucinationCheck)

def check_hallucination(state: RAGState) -> dict:
    result = hallucination_checker.invoke({
        "documents": "\n".join(state["documents"]),
        "generation": state["generation"]
    })
    return {"hallucination": result.hallucination}

# ── Node 5: Web Search Fallback ───────────────────────────
def web_search_fallback(state: RAGState) -> dict:
    print("🌐 Falling back to web search...")
    from langchain_community.tools.tavily_search import TavilySearchResults
    search = TavilySearchResults(max_results=3)
    results = search.invoke(state["question"])
    return {"documents": [r["content"] for r in results]}

# ── Routing Functions ─────────────────────────────────────
def route_after_grade(state: RAGState) -> Literal["generate", "web_search"]:
    if state["grade"] == "relevant":
        return "generate"
    return "web_search"  # No relevant docs → try web

def route_after_hallucination(state: RAGState) -> Literal["generate", END]:
    if state["hallucination"] == "no":
        return END        # Good answer, we're done
    if state["retry_count"] < 2:
        return "generate" # Retry generation
    return END            # Give up after 2 retries

# ── Build Graph ───────────────────────────────────────────
builder = StateGraph(RAGState)
builder.add_node("retrieve", retrieve)
builder.add_node("grade_documents", grade_documents)
builder.add_node("generate", generate)
builder.add_node("check_hallucination", check_hallucination)
builder.add_node("web_search", web_search_fallback)

builder.add_edge(START, "retrieve")
builder.add_edge("retrieve", "grade_documents")
builder.add_conditional_edges("grade_documents", route_after_grade)
builder.add_edge("generate", "check_hallucination")
builder.add_conditional_edges("check_hallucination", route_after_hallucination)
builder.add_edge("web_search", "grade_documents")

graph = builder.compile()

result = graph.invoke({
    "question": "What are the company's leave policies?",
    "documents": [], "generation": "", "grade": "",
    "hallucination": "", "retry_count": 0
})
print(result["generation"])
🎯 Advanced Retrieval Strategies Advanced
Hybrid search, MMR, re-ranking, and multi-query retrieval for production-grade accuracy.
advancedcoderag
▼
Retrieval Strategy Comparison
StrategyHow It WorksBest For
Similarity SearchPure cosine similarity on embeddingsBaseline, semantic queries
MMRMax Marginal Relevance — diverse resultsAvoiding duplicate chunks
BM25Keyword matching (TF-IDF)Exact term matching
Hybrid (RRF)Combine BM25 + semantic scoresBest overall accuracy ✅
Multi-QueryLLM generates multiple query variantsAmbiguous questions
Re-rankingCross-encoder re-scores initial resultsTop-K precision critical
Parent-DocRetrieve small chunks, return parentContext preservation
HyDELLM generates hypothetical doc, embed itSparse queries, hard questions
python
from langchain.retrievers import (
    MultiQueryRetriever,
    ContextualCompressionRetriever,
    EnsembleRetriever,
    ParentDocumentRetriever,
)
from langchain_community.retrievers import BM25Retriever
from langchain.retrievers.document_compressors import CrossEncoderReranker
from langchain_community.cross_encoders import HuggingFaceCrossEncoder

# ── 1. Multi-Query Retrieval ──────────────────────────────
multi_query = MultiQueryRetriever.from_llm(
    retriever=vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 4}),
    llm=llm,
    # LLM generates 3 query variants automatically
)

# ── 2. Hybrid (Ensemble) Retrieval — Best Default ─────────
bm25_retriever = BM25Retriever.from_documents(chunks)
bm25_retriever.k = 4

semantic_retriever = vectorstore.as_retriever(search_kwargs={"k": 4})

hybrid_retriever = EnsembleRetriever(
    retrievers=[bm25_retriever, semantic_retriever],
    weights=[0.4, 0.6],   # 40% keyword, 60% semantic
)

# ── 3. Re-ranking ─────────────────────────────────────────
encoder = HuggingFaceCrossEncoder(model_name="BAAI/bge-reranker-base")
reranker = CrossEncoderReranker(model=encoder, top_n=4)

reranking_retriever = ContextualCompressionRetriever(
    base_compressor=reranker,
    base_retriever=hybrid_retriever,  # Re-rank hybrid results
)

# ── 4. HyDE (Hypothetical Document Embeddings) ────────────
from langchain.chains import HypotheticalDocumentEmbedder

hyde_embeddings = HypotheticalDocumentEmbedder.from_llm(
    llm=llm,
    base_embeddings=OpenAIEmbeddings(),
    custom_instructions="Generate a detailed technical document answering: {query}"
)
hyde_vectorstore = Chroma(embedding_function=hyde_embeddings, persist_directory="./chroma_db")

# ── 5. Contextual Compression ─────────────────────────────
from langchain.retrievers.document_compressors import LLMChainExtractor
compressor = LLMChainExtractor.from_llm(llm)
compression_retriever = ContextualCompressionRetriever(
    base_compressor=compressor,
    base_retriever=semantic_retriever,
    # Extracts only the relevant parts of each chunk
)
🔄 Query Transformation Techniques
🔄 Query Transformation Techniques Advanced
Improve retrieval dramatically by transforming the user's query before searching — HyDE, step-back, sub-question decomposition.
advancedcoderaglangchain
▼
TechniqueHow It WorksBest For
Query RewritingLLM rephrases query to be clearer / more specificVague or conversational queries
HyDEGenerate a fake answer, embed that instead of the querySparse queries, hypothetical questions
Step-BackRetrieve on a broader version of the questionQuestions needing background context
Sub-Question Decomp.Break complex question into simpler sub-questionsMulti-hop questions, comparisons
Multi-QueryGenerate N variants, retrieve for all, mergeImproving recall ✅
python
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
from typing import List

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1:8b", temperature=0)

# ── 1. Query Rewriting ────────────────────────────────────
rewrite_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
You are improving a search query for a vector database.
Rewrite the following user question to be more specific and search-friendly.
Focus on key concepts. Return ONLY the rewritten query.

Original: {question}
Rewritten:""")

rewrite_chain = rewrite_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()

# "tell me stuff about rag" → "Retrieval-Augmented Generation RAG benefits and architecture"
better_query = rewrite_chain.invoke({"question": "tell me stuff about rag"})

# ── 2. HyDE — Hypothetical Document Embeddings ────────────
hyde_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Write a short, detailed paragraph that would ANSWER the following question.
Write it as if it's from a real document, even if you're guessing.
This will be used for semantic search.

Question: {question}
Hypothetical Answer Document:""")

hyde_chain = hyde_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()

# Embed the hypothetical answer, not the question
hypothetical_doc = hyde_chain.invoke({"question": "What is the leave policy?"})
# Use this for retrieval:
docs = retriever.invoke(hypothetical_doc)

# ── 3. Sub-Question Decomposition ─────────────────────────
class SubQuestions(BaseModel):
    questions: List[str] = Field(
        description="2-4 simpler sub-questions that together answer the original"
    )

decompose_llm = llm.with_structured_output(SubQuestions)

decompose_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
Break down this complex question into 2-4 simpler sub-questions
that can each be answered independently.

Question: {question}""")

def decompose_and_retrieve(question: str) -> str:
    # Get sub-questions
    result = (decompose_prompt | decompose_llm).invoke({"question": question})
    
    # Retrieve for each sub-question
    all_docs = []
    for sub_q in result.questions:
        docs = retriever.invoke(sub_q)
        all_docs.extend(docs)
    
    # Deduplicate by content
    seen = set()
    unique_docs = []
    for doc in all_docs:
        if doc.page_content not in seen:
            seen.add(doc.page_content)
            unique_docs.append(doc)
    
    return format_docs(unique_docs)

context = decompose_and_retrieve(
    "Compare the leave policies for full-time and part-time employees, including sick leave."
)

# ── 4. Multi-Query with Deduplication ────────────────────
class MultiQuery(BaseModel):
    queries: List[str] = Field(description="5 different ways to search for this information")

multi_q_llm = llm.with_structured_output(MultiQuery)

def multi_query_retrieve(question: str) -> List:
    result = multi_q_llm.invoke(
        f"Generate 5 different search queries to find information about: {question}"
    )
    all_docs = []
    for q in result.queries:
        all_docs.extend(retriever.invoke(q))
    
    # Deduplicate
    return list({doc.page_content: doc for doc in all_docs}.values())
⚡ Async Patterns
⚡ Async LangGraph & LangChain Patterns Intermediate
Write fully async agents for high throughput — async nodes, parallel execution, and async FastAPI integration.
intermediatecodeasyncops
▼
💡
Why async?Synchronous agents block the entire server thread while waiting for LLM responses (often 2–10s). Async allows handling hundreds of concurrent users with the same server resources.
python
import asyncio
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from typing import TypedDict, Annotated, List
from langchain_core.messages import BaseMessage
from langgraph.graph.message import add_messages

# ── Async nodes ───────────────────────────────────────────
class State(TypedDict):
    messages: Annotated[List[BaseMessage], add_messages]

async def async_llm_node(state: State) -> dict:
    """Async node — doesn't block event loop."""
    response = await llm.ainvoke(state["messages"])
    return {"messages": [response]}

async def async_retriever_node(state: State) -> dict:
    """Async retrieval."""
    query = state["messages"][-1].content
    docs = await retriever.ainvoke(query)
    context = format_docs(docs)
    return {"messages": [("system", f"Context:\n{context}")]}

# Build graph with async nodes (exactly the same API)
builder = StateGraph(State)
builder.add_node("retrieve", async_retriever_node)
builder.add_node("answer", async_llm_node)
builder.add_edge(START, "retrieve")
builder.add_edge("retrieve", "answer")
builder.add_edge("answer", END)
graph = builder.compile(checkpointer=MemorySaver())

# ── Run async ─────────────────────────────────────────────
async def main():
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "async-1"}}
    result = await graph.ainvoke(
        {"messages": [HumanMessage(content="What is RAG?")]},
        config=config,
    )
    print(result["messages"][-1].content)

asyncio.run(main())

# ── Parallel LLM calls ────────────────────────────────────
async def parallel_analysis(text: str) -> dict:
    """Run 3 LLM analyses concurrently — 3x faster than sequential."""
    tasks = [
        llm.ainvoke(f"Summarize: {text}"),
        llm.ainvoke(f"Extract key entities from: {text}"),
        llm.ainvoke(f"What is the sentiment of: {text}? (positive/negative/neutral)"),
    ]
    summary, entities, sentiment = await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
    return {
        "summary": summary.content,
        "entities": entities.content,
        "sentiment": sentiment.content,
    }

# ── LangGraph parallel nodes with Send() ─────────────────
from langgraph.constants import Send

def fan_out_node(state):
    """Fan out: send each document to a parallel node."""
    return [
        Send("process_doc", {"doc": doc, "question": state["question"]})
        for doc in state["documents"]
    ]

# In graph:
# builder.add_conditional_edges("retrieve", fan_out_node)
# → All documents processed in parallel, results merged

# ── Concurrent sessions (batch) ───────────────────────────
async def process_batch(questions: list) -> list:
    configs = [{"configurable": {"thread_id": f"batch-{i}"}} for i in range(len(questions))]
    tasks = [
        graph.ainvoke({"messages": [HumanMessage(content=q)]}, config=c)
        for q, c in zip(questions, configs)
    ]
    results = await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
    return [r["messages"][-1].content if not isinstance(r, Exception) else str(r) for r in results]
🗃️ SQL & Structured Data Agents
🗃️ SQL Agent — Natural Language to SQL Intermediate
Let users query SQL databases with plain English — the LLM writes and executes the SQL safely.
intermediatecodeagentlangchain
▼
🔐
Safety firstAlways use a read-only DB user for SQL agents. Never give an agent write access unless you have a human-approval gate. Validate and sanitize all generated SQL before execution in production.
python
from langchain_community.utilities import SQLDatabase
from langchain_community.agent_toolkits import SQLDatabaseToolkit
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

# ── Setup Database Connection ─────────────────────────────
# SQLite (perfect for local dev)
db = SQLDatabase.from_uri("sqlite:///./company_data.db")

# PostgreSQL
# db = SQLDatabase.from_uri("postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/mydb")

# Inspect what tables exist
print(db.get_table_names())
print(db.get_table_info())  # Schema for all tables

# ── Method 1: Simple Text-to-SQL Chain ────────────────────
sql_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", """You are a SQL expert. Given a question and database schema,
write a correct {dialect} SQL query to answer it.
Return ONLY the SQL query, no explanation.

Database schema:
{schema}"""),
    ("human", "{question}"),
])

def run_sql_chain(question: str) -> str:
    # Step 1: Generate SQL
    sql = (sql_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()).invoke({
        "dialect": db.dialect,
        "schema": db.get_table_info(),
        "question": question,
    })
    sql = sql.strip().strip("```sql").strip("```").strip()

    # Step 2: Execute SQL (read-only)
    try:
        result = db.run(sql)
        return f"SQL: {sql}\n\nResult: {result}"
    except Exception as e:
        return f"SQL Error: {e}\nSQL was: {sql}"

print(run_sql_chain("How many employees joined in 2024?"))

# ── Method 2: LangGraph SQL Agent (with correction loop) ──
class SQLState(TypedDict):
    question: str
    schema: str
    sql: str
    result: str
    error: str
    attempts: int

def generate_sql_node(state: SQLState) -> dict:
    error_context = f"\nPrevious error: {state['error']}\nFix the SQL." if state.get("error") else ""
    sql = (sql_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()).invoke({
        "dialect": db.dialect,
        "schema": state["schema"],
        "question": state["question"] + error_context,
    })
    return {"sql": sql.strip().strip("```sql").strip("```").strip(), "error": ""}

def execute_sql_node(state: SQLState) -> dict:
    try:
        result = db.run(state["sql"])
        return {"result": result, "error": ""}
    except Exception as e:
        return {"result": "", "error": str(e)}

def format_answer_node(state: SQLState) -> dict:
    answer_prompt = f"""
Question: {state['question']}
SQL Used: {state['sql']}
Raw Result: {state['result']}

Provide a clear, natural language answer."""
    answer = llm.invoke(answer_prompt).content
    return {"result": answer}

def should_retry(state: SQLState):
    if state.get("error") and state.get("attempts", 0) < 2:
        return "retry"
    if state.get("error"):
        return "give_up"
    return "format"

sql_builder = StateGraph(SQLState)
sql_builder.add_node("generate", generate_sql_node)
sql_builder.add_node("execute", execute_sql_node)
sql_builder.add_node("format_answer", format_answer_node)

sql_builder.add_edge(START, "generate")
sql_builder.add_edge("generate", "execute")
sql_builder.add_conditional_edges("execute", should_retry, {
    "retry": "generate",
    "give_up": END,
    "format": "format_answer",
})
sql_builder.add_edge("format_answer", END)

sql_graph = sql_builder.compile()
📊 CSV & Pandas DataFrame Agent Intermediate
Analyze CSV files and DataFrames using natural language — the LLM writes Python/Pandas code to answer questions.
intermediatecodelangchainagent
▼
python
import pandas as pd
from langchain_core.tools import tool
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate
from langchain_core.output_parsers import StrOutputParser
from io import StringIO

# Load your data
df = pd.read_csv("sales_data.csv")

# ── Approach 1: Describe schema, let LLM write Pandas code ─
def pandas_agent(question: str, df: pd.DataFrame) -> str:
    schema = f"""
DataFrame shape: {df.shape}
Columns: {list(df.columns)}
Dtypes: {df.dtypes.to_dict()}
Sample (3 rows):
{df.head(3).to_string()}
"""

    code_prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_template("""
You are a Pandas expert. Answer the question by writing Python code.
Use the variable `df` (already loaded). Return ONLY the code, no markdown.
The last line should be a print() statement with the answer.

DataFrame info:
{schema}

Question: {question}
Code:""")

    code = (code_prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()).invoke({
        "schema": schema, "question": question
    })

    # Execute in restricted environment
    local_vars = {"df": df, "pd": pd}
    import io, sys
    captured = io.StringIO()
    sys.stdout = captured
    try:
        exec(code.strip(), {"__builtins__": {"print": print, "len": len, "range": range}}, local_vars)
        output = captured.getvalue()
    except Exception as e:
        output = f"Error: {e}\nCode: {code}"
    finally:
        sys.stdout = sys.__stdout__
    return output

# ── Approach 2: Predefined safe analysis tools ────────────
@tool
def get_summary_stats(column: str) -> str:
    """Get summary statistics for a numeric column in the dataset.
    Args: column: Column name to summarize.
    """
    if column not in df.columns:
        return f"Column '{column}' not found. Available: {list(df.columns)}"
    return df[column].describe().to_string()

@tool
def filter_and_count(column: str, value: str) -> str:
    """Count rows where a column equals a specific value.
    Args: column: Column to filter on. value: Value to match.
    """
    count = len(df[df[column].astype(str) == value])
    return f"Rows where {column}='{value}': {count}"

@tool
def get_top_n(column: str, n: int = 5) -> str:
    """Get the top N most frequent values in a column.
    Args: column: Column name. n: Number of top values to return.
    """
    return df[column].value_counts().head(n).to_string()

# Use these safe tools in a ReAct agent
data_agent = create_react_agent(
    model=llm,
    tools=[get_summary_stats, filter_and_count, get_top_n],
    state_modifier="You are a data analyst. Use the tools to answer questions about the dataset."
)

result = data_agent.invoke({"messages": [HumanMessage("What are the top 5 product categories by count?")]})
👥 Multi-Agent Systems
👑 Supervisor Multi-Agent Pattern Advanced
A supervisor LLM routes tasks to specialist agents, collects results, and synthesizes the final output.
advancedenterprisecodeagentpattern
▼
👑
Supervisor
Decides routing
🔍
Researcher
Web + RAG search
✍️
Writer
Content creation
🧮
Analyst
Data analysis
python
from langgraph_supervisor import create_supervisor  # pip install langgraph-supervisor
from langgraph.prebuilt import create_react_agent
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI

llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o")
fast_llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o-mini")

# ── Define Specialist Agents ───────────────────────────────
researcher = create_react_agent(
    model=llm,
    tools=[search_web, retriever_tool],
    name="researcher",
    prompt="You are a research specialist. Find accurate, up-to-date information. Always cite sources."
)

writer = create_react_agent(
    model=fast_llm,
    tools=[],  # No tools needed — receives research context
    name="writer",
    prompt="You are a skilled technical writer. Transform research into clear, engaging content."
)

analyst = create_react_agent(
    model=llm,
    tools=[calculator, db_tool],
    name="analyst",
    prompt="You are a data analyst. Perform calculations, query data, and provide quantitative insights."
)

# ── Create Supervisor ──────────────────────────────────────
supervisor_graph = create_supervisor(
    agents=[researcher, writer, analyst],
    model=llm,
    prompt="""You are a project supervisor. Delegate tasks to specialists:
    - researcher: For fact-finding, web search, document retrieval
    - writer: For writing, editing, summarizing (after researcher gathers info)  
    - analyst: For calculations, data queries, quantitative analysis
    
    Always: 1) Research first, 2) Analyze data if needed, 3) Write the final output.
    When all tasks are complete, summarize the findings."""
)

graph = supervisor_graph.compile(checkpointer=MemorySaver())

# Run the supervisor
result = graph.invoke({
    "messages": [HumanMessage("Research AI adoption in healthcare, analyze market size numbers, and write an executive summary.")]
}, config={"configurable": {"thread_id": "exec-report-1"}})
⚠️
Multi-Agent Cost WarningEach agent invocation can make multiple LLM calls. A 3-agent supervisor system with 5 tool calls each = 15+ LLM calls per request. Profile costs early and use smaller models for specialist agents.
🧩 Subgraphs — Composable Agent Modules Advanced
Embed entire graphs as nodes in parent graphs. The key to building modular, reusable enterprise agents.
advancedenterprisecodelanggraph
▼
python
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, START, END
from typing import TypedDict

# ── Subgraph: RAG Pipeline ────────────────────────────────
class RAGSubState(TypedDict):
    query: str
    context: str         # Output this subgraph produces

def rag_retrieve(state: RAGSubState) -> dict:
    docs = retriever.invoke(state["query"])
    return {"context": "\n".join(d.page_content for d in docs)}

rag_builder = StateGraph(RAGSubState)
rag_builder.add_node("retrieve", rag_retrieve)
rag_builder.add_edge(START, "retrieve")
rag_builder.add_edge("retrieve", END)
rag_subgraph = rag_builder.compile()  # Compiled subgraph

# ── Parent Graph ──────────────────────────────────────────
class ParentState(TypedDict):
    user_question: str
    context: str          # Will be populated by subgraph
    final_answer: str

def prepare_query(state: ParentState) -> dict:
    """Preprocess the question before RAG."""
    # Could expand/clean the query here
    return {}

def call_rag(state: ParentState) -> dict:
    """Invoke the RAG subgraph."""
    # State key mapping: parent "user_question" → subgraph "query"
    result = rag_subgraph.invoke({"query": state["user_question"]})
    return {"context": result["context"]}

def generate_answer(state: ParentState) -> dict:
    prompt = f"Context: {state['context']}\nQuestion: {state['user_question']}"
    answer = llm.invoke(prompt).content
    return {"final_answer": answer}

# Build parent graph
parent_builder = StateGraph(ParentState)
parent_builder.add_node("prepare", prepare_query)
parent_builder.add_node("rag", call_rag)         # Calls subgraph internally
parent_builder.add_node("generate", generate_answer)

parent_builder.add_edge(START, "prepare")
parent_builder.add_edge("prepare", "rag")
parent_builder.add_edge("rag", "generate")
parent_builder.add_edge("generate", END)

parent_graph = parent_builder.compile()
🔗 MCP — Model Context Protocol
🔗 What is MCP? Model Context Protocol Overview Advanced
MCP is an open standard (by Anthropic) for connecting LLMs to external data sources and tools via a unified, language-agnostic protocol.
advancedtoolspattern
▼
🔗
MCP in one sentenceModel Context Protocol defines a standard way for LLM applications to connect to external tools, files, databases, and APIs — like a USB-C standard for AI integrations. Any MCP host can use any MCP server.
Architecture
🧠
MCP Host
Claude / your LangGraph app
↔
📄
MCP Client
Protocol layer (SDK)
↔
🔸
MCP Server
Exposes tools, resources, prompts
↔
🌐
Data Sources
Files, DBs, APIs, services
MCP vs Custom @tool — When to Use Each
AspectCustom @toolMCP Server
ReusabilitySingle agent / codebaseAny MCP-compatible host
LanguagePython onlyAny language (Python, JS, Go, Rust…)
DiscoveryHardcoded at build timeDynamic — server advertises tools at runtime
DeploymentIn-process (same app)Separate process or remote service
Use whenSimple, project-specific toolsShared tools across many agents / teams
Transport Options
stdio
Local process via stdin/stdout. Best for local tools (filesystem, local DB). Used in Claude Desktop.
HTTP + SSE
Remote server over HTTP. Best for shared services and multi-user deployments.
WebSocket
Bi-directional streaming. Best for real-time and long-running tools.
In-process
Same Python process. Best for testing and development.
🔸 Building an MCP Server in Python (FastMCP) Advanced
Implement a fully compliant MCP server exposing tools, resources, and prompt templates — usable from Claude Desktop, LangGraph, or any MCP client.
advancedcodetools
▼
bash
pip install mcp fastmcp httpx
python
# server.py  -- MCP Server using FastMCP (high-level SDK)
from fastmcp import FastMCP
import httpx, json
from pathlib import Path

mcp = FastMCP("My Agent Tools")

# -- Tool: web fetch --------------------------------------------------
@mcp.tool()
async def fetch_url(url: str) -> str:
    """Fetch the content of a web page and return as text (max 5000 chars)."""
    async with httpx.AsyncClient() as client:
        resp = await client.get(url, follow_redirects=True, timeout=15)
        return resp.text[:5000]

# -- Tool: read local file --------------------------------------------
@mcp.tool()
def read_file(path: str) -> str:
    """Read a local file and return its text content."""
    p = Path(path)
    if not p.exists():
        return f"Error: File not found: {path}"
    if p.stat().st_size > 100_000:
        return "Error: File too large (>100KB)"
    return p.read_text()

# -- Tool: search your RAG knowledge base ----------------------------
@mcp.tool()
def search_knowledge_base(query: str, top_k: int = 5) -> str:
    """Search the internal knowledge base for relevant documents."""
    from my_rag import retriever  # your LangChain retriever
    docs = retriever.invoke(query)[:top_k]
    return "\n\n".join(
        f"[{d.metadata.get('source', '?')}]\n{d.page_content}"
        for d in docs
    )

# -- Resource: expose configuration data ----------------------------
@mcp.resource("config://app")
def get_app_config() -> str:
    """Application configuration available to the agent."""
    return json.dumps({"version": "1.0", "env": "production"})

# -- Prompt template: reusable system prompt -------------------------
@mcp.prompt()
def rag_system_prompt(topic: str) -> str:
    """System prompt for RAG over a specific topic."""
    return (f"You are an expert assistant on {topic}. "
            "Use only the retrieved documents to answer. "
            "Always cite your sources.")

# -- Run the server --------------------------------------------------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # stdio transport: works with Claude Desktop and local agents
    mcp.run()

    # HTTP transport: for remote / multi-user access
    # mcp.run(transport="http", host="0.0.0.0", port=8000)
Claude Desktop Configuration
