🛡️ Error Handling & Retry Logic Intermediate
Make your agents resilient with structured error handling, retries, and graceful fallbacks.
intermediateenterprisecodepattern
▼
python
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableWithFallbacks
from langchain_openai import ChatOpenAI
from langchain_anthropic import ChatAnthropic
import time, logging

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── Pattern 1: LLM Fallbacks ──────────────────────────────
primary_llm = ChatOpenAI(model="gpt-4o")
fallback_llm = ChatAnthropic(model="claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022")

# Automatically falls back to Claude if GPT-4o fails
resilient_llm = primary_llm.with_fallbacks([fallback_llm])

# ── Pattern 2: Retry with Exponential Backoff ─────────────
from tenacity import retry, stop_after_attempt, wait_exponential, retry_if_exception_type
from openai import RateLimitError, APIError

@retry(
    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
    wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, min=4, max=60),
    retry=retry_if_exception_type((RateLimitError, APIError)),
)
def call_llm_with_retry(messages):
    return llm.invoke(messages)

# ── Pattern 3: Error handling in LangGraph nodes ──────────
def safe_llm_node(state: AgentState) -> dict:
    """Node with built-in error handling."""
    try:
        response = resilient_llm.invoke(state["messages"])
        return {"messages": [response], "error": None}
    except Exception as e:
        logger.error(f"LLM call failed: {e}")
        # Return error state instead of crashing
        return {
            "error": str(e),
            "messages": [AIMessage(content="I encountered an error. Let me try a different approach.")]
        }

def route_on_error(state: AgentState) -> Literal["retry", "fallback", END]:
    if state.get("error") and state.get("retry_count", 0) < 2:
        return "retry"
    elif state.get("error"):
        return "fallback"
    return END

# ── Pattern 4: Timeout handling ───────────────────────────
import asyncio
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableConfig

async def node_with_timeout(state: AgentState) -> dict:
    try:
        result = await asyncio.wait_for(
            llm.ainvoke(state["messages"]),
            timeout=30.0  # 30 second timeout
        )
        return {"messages": [result]}
    except asyncio.TimeoutError:
        return {"error": "Request timed out", "messages": []}
🔐 Security & Safety
🔐 Security: Prompt Injection & Guardrails Enterprise
Defend against prompt injection, jailbreaks, and data leakage in production agent systems.
enterpriseadvancedsecurity
▼
🚨
Top Security Threats for AI Agents1) Prompt Injection — malicious content in retrieved docs or user input. 2) Data Exfiltration — agent leaks sensitive context. 3) Tool Abuse — agent executes unintended tool calls. 4) PII Leakage — LLM logs or traces contain sensitive data.
python
from langchain_core.messages import SystemMessage
import re

# ── 1. Input Sanitization ─────────────────────────────────
def sanitize_user_input(user_input: str) -> str:
    """Remove injection patterns from user input."""
    # Block common injection attempts
    injection_patterns = [
        r"ignore previous instructions",
        r"disregard.*system.*prompt",
        r"you are now",
        r"pretend.*you are",
        r"\\n\\n(Human|Assistant|User|System):",
        r"<\|.*\|>",  # Token injection
    ]
    for pattern in injection_patterns:
        if re.search(pattern, user_input, re.IGNORECASE):
            raise ValueError("Potentially malicious input detected")
    
    # Sanitize special characters
    return user_input[:2000]  # Length limit

# ── 2. Output Guardrails ──────────────────────────────────
from pydantic import BaseModel, validator

class SafeAgentOutput(BaseModel):
    answer: str
    contains_pii: bool = False
    
    @validator("answer")
    def check_no_secrets(cls, v):
        secret_patterns = [r"\b[A-Z0-9]{20,}\b", r"sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]+"]
        for pattern in secret_patterns:
            if re.search(pattern, v):
                raise ValueError("Output contains potential secrets")
        return v

# ── 3. NeMo Guardrails (Nvidia) ──────────────────────────
# pip install nemoguardrails
from nemoguardrails import RailsConfig, LLMRails

config = RailsConfig.from_path("./guardrails_config/")
rails = LLMRails(config)

# Define rails in COLANG files:
# define user ask about competitors
#   "what about CompetitorX?"
#   "tell me about your rivals"
#
# define bot refuse to discuss competitors
#   "I can only discuss our own products."
#
# define flow user ask about competitors
#   user ask about competitors
#   bot refuse to discuss competitors

# ── 4. PII Detection & Redaction ─────────────────────────
import presidio_analyzer
from presidio_analyzer import AnalyzerEngine
from presidio_anonymizer import AnonymizerEngine

analyzer = AnalyzerEngine()
anonymizer = AnonymizerEngine()

def redact_pii(text: str) -> str:
    results = analyzer.analyze(text=text, language="en")
    anonymized = anonymizer.anonymize(text=text, analyzer_results=results)
    return anonymized.text  # Replaces names, emails, SSNs, etc.

# ── 5. Tool Permission System ─────────────────────────────
TOOL_PERMISSIONS = {
    "readonly_user": ["search_knowledge_base", "calculator"],
    "editor": ["search_knowledge_base", "calculator", "update_document"],
    "admin": ["search_knowledge_base", "calculator", "update_document", "delete_record"],
}

def get_allowed_tools(user_role: str, all_tools: list) -> list:
    allowed_names = TOOL_PERMISSIONS.get(user_role, [])
    return [t for t in all_tools if t.name in allowed_names]

Option B: FastAPI Wrapper (custom control)
python
from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, Depends
from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
from fastapi.security import HTTPBearer
from pydantic import BaseModel
from typing import AsyncGenerator
import json

app = FastAPI(title="RAG Agent API", version="2.0.0")
security = HTTPBearer()

class QueryRequest(BaseModel):
    question: str
    thread_id: str = "default"
    user_id: str = ""

class QueryResponse(BaseModel):
    answer: str
    sources: list[str]
    thread_id: str

# Load compiled graph once at startup
from src.graphs.main_graph import graph

@app.post("/query", response_model=QueryResponse)
async def query(request: QueryRequest, token=Depends(security)):
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": request.thread_id}}
    try:
        result = await graph.ainvoke(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=request.question)]},
            config=config
        )
        return QueryResponse(
            answer=result["messages"][-1].content,
            sources=result.get("sources", []),
            thread_id=request.thread_id
        )
    except Exception as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=str(e))

@app.post("/query/stream")
async def query_stream(request: QueryRequest):
    config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": request.thread_id}}
    
    async def event_generator() -> AsyncGenerator[str, None]:
        async for chunk in graph.astream(
            {"messages": [HumanMessage(content=request.question)]},
            config=config,
            stream_mode="messages"
        ):
            if chunk[1].get("langgraph_node") == "generate":
                data = json.dumps({"token": chunk[0].content})
                yield f"data: {data}\n\n"
        yield "data: [DONE]\n\n"
    
    return StreamingResponse(event_generator(), media_type="text/event-stream")

@app.get("/health")
async def health(): return {"status": "ok"}

🛢 Production Hardening
🛢 Graceful Shutdown Advanced
Drain in-flight LangGraph invocations before the process exits — prevents corrupted state and dropped responses in containerised deployments.
advancedenterpriseproduction
▼
💡
Why it matters — Kubernetes sends SIGTERM before killing a pod. Without a shutdown hook, in-flight graph invocations are killed mid-stream, leaving checkpointed state half-written and clients with broken connections.
FastAPI + Uvicorn graceful shutdown
python
# app/main.py
import asyncio, signal, uvicorn
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from fastapi import FastAPI
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama
from langgraph.graph import StateGraph, MessagesState

# Track active tasks so we can drain them
active_tasks: set[asyncio.Task] = set()

@asynccontextmanager
async def lifespan(app: FastAPI):
    # ── Startup ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    app.state.llm   = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1")
    app.state.graph = build_graph(app.state.llm)
    print("✓ App started")
    yield
    # ── Shutdown — drain in-flight tasks ─────────────────────────
    print(f"Draining {len(active_tasks)} active task(s)…")
    if active_tasks:
        await asyncio.wait(active_tasks, timeout=30)   # 30 s drain window
    print("✓ Clean shutdown complete")

app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan)

@app.post("/invoke")
async def invoke(payload: dict):
    task = asyncio.current_task()
    active_tasks.add(task)
    try:
        result = await app.state.graph.ainvoke(
            {"messages": [("user", payload["message"])]},
            config={"configurable": {"thread_id": payload.get("thread_id","default")}}
        )
        return {"response": result["messages"][-1].content}
    finally:
        active_tasks.discard(task)

def build_graph(llm):
    builder = StateGraph(MessagesState)
    builder.add_node("chat", lambda s: {"messages": [llm.invoke(s["messages"])]})
    builder.set_entry_point("chat")
    builder.set_finish_point("chat")
    return builder.compile()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    uvicorn.run(
        "main:app",
        host="0.0.0.0", port=8000,
        workers=1,                     # use 1 worker with async; scale via replicas
        timeout_graceful_shutdown=35,  # gives 35 s after SIGTERM before SIGKILL
    )
Plain asyncio / script
python
import asyncio, signal

shutdown_event = asyncio.Event()

def _signal_handler():
    print("SIGTERM received — starting graceful shutdown")
    shutdown_event.set()

async def main():
    loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    loop.add_signal_handler(signal.SIGTERM, _signal_handler)
    loop.add_signal_handler(signal.SIGINT,  _signal_handler)

    # Your server / graph loop here
    await shutdown_event.wait()
    # cleanup: flush queues, close DB connections, etc.
    print("Shutdown complete")

asyncio.run(main())
❤️ Health Check Endpoints Advanced
Liveness and readiness probes Kubernetes actually relies on — including an Ollama ping so the pod is not marked ready until the model is loaded.
advancedenterpriseproduction
▼
python
# app/health.py
import time, httpx
from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, status

router = APIRouter()
_start_time = time.time()

@router.get("/healthz", tags=["ops"])
async def liveness():
    """Kubernetes liveness probe — is the process alive?"""
    return {"status": "ok", "uptime_s": round(time.time() - _start_time, 1)}

@router.get("/readyz", tags=["ops"])
async def readiness(request):
    """Kubernetes readiness probe — is the app ready to serve traffic?
    Checks: Ollama reachable + model loaded."""
    checks = {}

    # 1. Ollama reachable
    try:
        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=3) as client:
            r = await client.get("http://localhost:11434/api/tags")
            r.raise_for_status()
        checks["ollama"] = "ok"
    except Exception as e:
        checks["ollama"] = f"error: {e}"

    # 2. LLM object initialised
    llm = getattr(getattr(request, "app", None), "state", None)
    checks["llm_ready"] = "ok" if (llm and hasattr(llm, "llm")) else "not_ready"

    failed = [k for k, v in checks.items() if v != "ok"]
    if failed:
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,
            detail={"status": "not_ready", "checks": checks}
        )
    return {"status": "ready", "checks": checks}
Kubernetes probe config
yaml
# k8s/deployment.yaml  (probe section only)
livenessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /healthz
    port: 8000
  initialDelaySeconds: 10
  periodSeconds: 15
  failureThreshold: 3

readinessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /readyz
    port: 8000
  initialDelaySeconds: 20   # allow model warm-up
  periodSeconds: 10
  failureThreshold: 3
⚡ Circuit Breakers for LLM Outages Advanced
Stop cascading failures when Ollama is slow or unreachable — circuit breaker pattern with tenacity retries and a graceful fallback response.
advancedenterpriseproduction
▼
bash
pip install tenacity
python
# app/resilience.py
import asyncio, enum, time, logging
from tenacity import (
    retry, stop_after_attempt, wait_exponential,
    retry_if_exception_type, before_sleep_log
)
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama
from langchain_core.messages import HumanMessage

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# ── Circuit breaker state machine ────────────────────────────────────
class State(enum.Enum):
    CLOSED   = "closed"    # normal — requests flow through
    OPEN     = "open"      # failing — reject immediately
    HALF_OPEN= "half_open" # testing — allow one probe request

class CircuitBreaker:
    def __init__(self, failure_threshold=3, recovery_timeout=30):
        self.state             = State.CLOSED
        self.failure_count     = 0
        self.failure_threshold = failure_threshold
        self.recovery_timeout  = recovery_timeout
        self._opened_at        = None

    def record_success(self):
        self.failure_count = 0
        self.state         = State.CLOSED

    def record_failure(self):
        self.failure_count += 1
        if self.failure_count >= self.failure_threshold:
            self.state      = State.OPEN
            self._opened_at = time.monotonic()
            logger.warning("Circuit OPEN — LLM requests blocked")

    def allow_request(self) -> bool:
        if self.state == State.CLOSED:
            return True
        if self.state == State.OPEN:
            elapsed = time.monotonic() - (self._opened_at or 0)
            if elapsed >= self.recovery_timeout:
                self.state = State.HALF_OPEN
                logger.info("Circuit HALF-OPEN — probing")
                return True
            return False
        return True  # HALF_OPEN: allow one probe

_cb = CircuitBreaker(failure_threshold=3, recovery_timeout=30)

# ── Retry decorator (wraps a single attempt) ─────────────────────────
@retry(
    retry=retry_if_exception_type((ConnectionError, TimeoutError, Exception)),
    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
    wait=wait_exponential(multiplier=1, min=1, max=10),
    before_sleep=before_sleep_log(logger, logging.WARNING),
    reraise=True,
)
async def _attempt_invoke(llm: ChatOllama, message: str) -> str:
    response = await asyncio.wait_for(
        llm.ainvoke([HumanMessage(content=message)]),
        timeout=30,
    )
    return response.content

# ── Public entry point ────────────────────────────────────────────────
FALLBACK_MSG = "I'm temporarily unavailable — please try again in a moment."

async def invoke_with_circuit_breaker(llm: ChatOllama, message: str) -> str:
    if not _cb.allow_request():
        logger.error("Circuit OPEN — returning fallback")
        return FALLBACK_MSG
    try:
        result = await _attempt_invoke(llm, message)
        _cb.record_success()
        return result
    except Exception as e:
        _cb.record_failure()
        logger.error(f"LLM call failed after retries: {e}")
        return FALLBACK_MSG
python
# Usage in a FastAPI route
@app.post("/chat")
async def chat(payload: dict):
    response = await invoke_with_circuit_breaker(
        app.state.llm, payload["message"]
    )
    return {"response": response}
📜 Structured Logging with Request IDs Advanced
JSON logs with a per-request correlation ID so every LangGraph step, LLM call, and error can be traced back to the originating request in any log aggregator.
advancedenterpriseproduction
▼
bash
pip install python-json-logger
python
# app/logging_config.py
import logging, uuid
from contextvars import ContextVar
from pythonjsonlogger import jsonlogger

# Context variable — automatically propagated through async tasks
request_id_var: ContextVar[str] = ContextVar("request_id", default="-")

class RequestIdFilter(logging.Filter):
    def filter(self, record):
        record.request_id = request_id_var.get("-")
        return True

def configure_logging(level: str = "INFO"):
    handler = logging.StreamHandler()
    fmt = jsonlogger.JsonFormatter(
        fmt="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s %(message)s %(request_id)s",
        datefmt="%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S",
    )
    handler.setFormatter(fmt)
    handler.addFilter(RequestIdFilter())

    root = logging.getLogger()
    root.handlers.clear()
    root.addHandler(handler)
    root.setLevel(getattr(logging, level.upper(), logging.INFO))
python
# app/middleware.py
import logging, uuid
from starlette.middleware.base import BaseHTTPMiddleware
from app.logging_config import request_id_var

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class RequestIdMiddleware(BaseHTTPMiddleware):
    async def dispatch(self, request, call_next):
        rid = request.headers.get("X-Request-ID") or str(uuid.uuid4())
        token = request_id_var.set(rid)
        request.state.request_id = rid
        try:
            logger.info(
                "request_started",
                extra={"method": request.method, "path": request.url.path}
            )
            response = await call_next(request)
            response.headers["X-Request-ID"] = rid
            logger.info("request_finished", extra={"status": response.status_code})
            return response
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.error("request_failed", exc_info=exc)
            raise
        finally:
            request_id_var.reset(token)
python
# app/main.py  (wire it up)
from app.logging_config import configure_logging
from app.middleware import RequestIdMiddleware

configure_logging("INFO")

app = FastAPI(lifespan=lifespan)
app.add_middleware(RequestIdMiddleware)

# In any route or graph node — logs automatically carry the request_id
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

async def my_graph_node(state):
    logger.info("node_entered", extra={"node": "my_graph_node", "msg_count": len(state["messages"])})
    # ... your logic

🐨 Docker & Container Configuration Advanced
Production-grade Dockerfile with multi-stage build, non-root user, health check, and a docker-compose that wires Ollama + the FastAPI app together correctly.
advancedenterpriseproduction
▼
Dockerfile (multi-stage, non-root)
dockerfile
# ── Stage 1: build/install deps ──────────────────────────────────────
FROM python:3.11-slim AS builder
WORKDIR /app
COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir --prefix=/install -r requirements.txt

# ── Stage 2: production image ─────────────────────────────────────────
FROM python:3.11-slim
WORKDIR /app

# Non-root user
RUN addgroup --system app && adduser --system --ingroup app app

COPY --from=builder /install /usr/local
COPY --chown=app:app . .

ENV PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 \
    PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1 \
    PORT=8000

USER app

HEALTHCHECK --interval=15s --timeout=5s --start-period=20s --retries=3 \
  CMD python -c "import httpx; httpx.get('http://localhost:8000/healthz').raise_for_status()"

EXPOSE 8000
CMD ["uvicorn", "app.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000", \
     "--timeout-graceful-shutdown", "35"]
docker-compose.yml
yaml
version: "3.9"
services:
  ollama:
    image: ollama/ollama:latest
    volumes:
      - ollama_data:/root/.ollama       # persist downloaded models
    ports:
      - "11434:11434"
    deploy:
      resources:
        reservations:
          devices:
            - driver: nvidia
              count: all
              capabilities: [gpu]       # remove if CPU-only
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "curl", "-f", "http://localhost:11434/api/tags"]
      interval: 10s
      retries: 5
      start_period: 30s

  # Pull the model on startup (runs once, then exits)
  model-puller:
    image: ollama/ollama:latest
    depends_on:
      ollama:
        condition: service_healthy
    entrypoint: >
      sh -c "ollama pull llama3.1 && ollama pull nomic-embed-text"
    environment:
      - OLLAMA_HOST=http://ollama:11434
    restart: "no"

  app:
    build: .
    depends_on:
      ollama:
        condition: service_healthy
    environment:
      - OLLAMA_BASE_URL=http://ollama:11434
      - LOG_LEVEL=INFO
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    restart: unless-stopped

volumes:
  ollama_data:
python
# Read Ollama URL from env (so it works locally AND in Docker)
import os
from langchain_ollama import ChatOllama, OllamaEmbeddings

OLLAMA_BASE_URL = os.getenv("OLLAMA_BASE_URL", "http://localhost:11434")

llm = ChatOllama(model="llama3.1", base_url=OLLAMA_BASE_URL)
embeddings = OllamaEmbeddings(model="nomic-embed-text", base_url=OLLAMA_BASE_URL)
⚠️
CPU-only deployment — remove the deploy.resources.reservations block from docker-compose and expect ~5–10x slower inference. Use smaller models (phi3.5:3.8b, gemma2:2b) for CPU-only containers.

📄 Research Papers — RAG, Agents & Reasoning Advanced
The foundational and cutting-edge papers behind every technique in this cheatsheet — with direct arXiv links.
advancedreferenceresearch
▼
Retrieval-Augmented Generation
PaperWhat it introducesLink
RAG (Lewis et al., 2020)The original RAG paper — retrieval + seq2seq generationarxiv.org/abs/2005.11401
Self-RAG (Asai et al., 2023)Model decides when to retrieve, reflects on its own outputarxiv.org/abs/2310.11511
Corrective RAG (Shi et al., 2024)Evaluates retrieved docs, falls back to web search if poorarxiv.org/abs/2401.15884
HyDE (Gao et al., 2022)Hypothetical Document Embeddings — generate then retrievearxiv.org/abs/2212.10496
RAGAS (Es et al., 2023)Reference-free RAG evaluation metrics (faithfulness, relevancy)arxiv.org/abs/2309.15217
Contextual Compression (Cohere, 2023)Filter retrieved chunks to only the relevant partstxt.cohere.com/rerank
Agents & Reasoning
PaperWhat it introducesLink
ReAct (Yao et al., 2022)Reasoning + Acting — the foundation of ReAct agentsarxiv.org/abs/2210.03629
Chain-of-Thought (Wei et al., 2022)Step-by-step reasoning improves complex task performancearxiv.org/abs/2201.11903
Toolformer (Schick et al., 2023)LLMs that learn to call APIs by themselvesarxiv.org/abs/2302.04761
Plan-and-Solve (Wang et al., 2023)Decompose task → solve each part — LangGraph Plan-Execute patternarxiv.org/abs/2305.04091
LATS (Zhou et al., 2023)Language Agent Tree Search — MCTS for LLM agentsarxiv.org/abs/2310.04406
Reflexion (Shinn et al., 2023)Agents reflect on failures and improve iterativelyarxiv.org/abs/2303.11366
Multimodal & Safety
PaperLink
LLaVA — Large Language and Vision Assistantarxiv.org/abs/2304.08485
Constitutional AI (Anthropic, 2022) — RLHF-free safetyarxiv.org/abs/2212.08073
Llama 3 Technical Report (Meta, 2024)arxiv.org/abs/2407.21783
Gemma 2 (Google, 2024)arxiv.org/abs/2408.00118

💡
Picking a model for local use
< 8GB RAM → phi3.5:3.8b, gemma2:2b, llama3.2:3b
8–16GB RAM → llama3.1:8b, mistral:7b, qwen2.5:7b
16–32GB RAM → llama3.1:70b-q4, mistral-nemo:12b
32GB+ RAM → llama3.3:70b, qwen2.5:72b
GPU (VRAM) → divide model size in GB by 1.5 to estimate VRAM needed for Q4

⚡ Quick Reference
⚡ Common Patterns Quick Reference Beginner
Copy-paste ready snippets for the most common LangGraph and RAG patterns.
beginnerintermediatecode
▼
Structured LLM Output (Pydantic)
python
from pydantic import BaseModel
class Answer(BaseModel):
    response: str
    confidence: float
    sources: list[str]

structured_llm = llm.with_structured_output(Answer)
result = structured_llm.invoke("What is RAG?")
print(result.confidence)  # 0.95
Streaming with LangGraph
python
for event in graph.stream(inputs, config, stream_mode="values"):
    print(event)  # Full state after each node

for event in graph.stream(inputs, config, stream_mode="updates"):
    print(event)  # Only what changed

# Token-level streaming
async for chunk in graph.astream_events(inputs, config, version="v2"):
    if chunk["event"] == "on_chat_model_stream":
        print(chunk["data"]["chunk"].content, end="", flush=True)
Prompt Templates
python
from langchain_core.prompts import ChatPromptTemplate, MessagesPlaceholder

# With chat history
prompt = ChatPromptTemplate.from_messages([
    ("system", "You are a helpful assistant. Context: {context}"),
    MessagesPlaceholder("chat_history"),  # Inject conversation history
    ("human", "{question}"),
])

chain = prompt | llm | StrOutputParser()
result = chain.invoke({
    "context": "...",
    "chat_history": state["messages"][:-1],
    "question": "Follow-up question"
})
Visualize Your Graph
python
# Mermaid diagram (paste at mermaid.live)
print(graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid())

# PNG image (requires graphviz + pygraphviz)
from IPython.display import Image
Image(graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid_png())
Batch Processing
python
questions = ["Q1", "Q2", "Q3", "Q4", "Q5"]
inputs = [{"question": q, "documents": [], "answer": ""} for q in questions]

# Process up to 5 in parallel
results = graph.batch(inputs, config={"max_concurrency": 5})
answers = [r["answer"] for r in results]
🔬 State Inspection & Management Intermediate
Commands for inspecting, updating, and navigating graph state — essential for debugging.
intermediatecodelanggraph
▼
python
config = {"configurable": {"thread_id": "my-thread"}}

# Get current state
state = graph.get_state(config)
print(state.values)        # Current state dict
print(state.next)          # Which node executes next
print(state.metadata)      # Step count, etc.

# Update state manually (e.g., to fix errors)
graph.update_state(
    config,
    {"answer": "Corrected answer", "retry_count": 0},
    as_node="generate"  # Treat as if 'generate' node produced this update
)

# Get full history
history = list(graph.get_state_history(config))
for snapshot in history:
    print(f"Step {snapshot.metadata['step']}: {snapshot.next}")

# Time-travel: resume from a past checkpoint
past_config = history[3].config  # 3 steps ago
graph.invoke(
    Command(resume={}),
    config=past_config
)

# Delete a thread (clear all checkpoints)
checkpointer.delete(config)
🐛 Troubleshooting
🐛 Common Issues & Fixes Beginner
Quick solutions for the most frequent LangGraph and RAG problems.
beginnerintermediate
▼
ProblemLikely CauseFix
Agent runs foreverNo termination conditionAdd recursion_limit to config. Check tools_condition routes to END.
Context window exceededToo many messages/docsTrim messages with keep_last_n reducer. Reduce chunk size or k.
Hallucination despite RAGLow-quality retrievalImprove chunking strategy. Add hallucination check node. Use hybrid retrieval.
Slow responsesSequential LLM callsUse Send() API for parallel nodes. Enable semantic caching.
Tool not being calledVague tool descriptionImprove description — be specific about WHEN and WHY to use it.
Rate limit errorsToo many API callsAdd retry with backoff. Use batching. Cache embeddings.
State not persistingNo checkpointerCompile graph with checkpointer=... and pass thread_id in config.
Wrong node orderEdge misconfigurationCall graph.get_graph().draw_mermaid() to visualize the actual graph.
Pydantic validation errorState schema mismatchEnsure all state fields have defaults or are always populated by START input.
Empty retrieval resultsEmbedding mismatchUse same embedding model for ingestion AND retrieval. Check collection name.
Debugging with Verbose Logging
python
import langchain
langchain.debug = True  # Verbose output for ALL LangChain calls

# Graph-level: stream each node's output
for step in graph.stream(inputs, config, stream_mode="updates"):
    node_name = list(step.keys())[0]
    print(f"\n{'='*40}")
    print(f"Node: {node_name}")
    print(f"Output: {step[node_name]}")

# Set recursion limit (default is 25)
result = graph.invoke(inputs, config={"recursion_limit": 10})
✅ Best Practices Checklist All Levels
Non-negotiable best practices for production LangGraph RAG agents.
beginnerenterprisepattern
▼
🏗️ Architecture
  • Design state schema before nodes
  • Use typed dicts + pydantic validation
  • Keep nodes small and focused (SRP)
  • Use subgraphs for reusable modules
  • Visualize graph before running
  • Never hardcode LLM responses
  • Don't use global state (use TypedDict)
📚 RAG Quality
  • Use hybrid retrieval (BM25 + semantic)
  • Add re-ranking for top-K precision
  • Preserve metadata (source, page, date)
  • Grade docs before generation
  • Evaluate with RAGAS regularly
  • Don't use chunk_overlap=0
  • Don't mix embedding models
🔐 Security
  • Sanitize all user inputs
  • Apply RBAC to tool access
  • Redact PII from logs/traces
  • Add human approval for destructive actions
  • Least-privilege for all tool APIs
  • Never log raw user queries in prod
  • Don't give agents root DB access
📡 Operations
  • Enable LangSmith from day 1
  • Set recursion_limit on all graphs
  • Use PostgresSaver in production
  • Cache embeddings & LLM responses
  • Monitor cost per request
  • Don't use MemorySaver in production
  • Don't ignore error states
🗺️ Recommended Learning Path Beginner
Step-by-step learning roadmap from zero to enterprise AI agent developer.
beginner
▼
1
Week 1-2: Foundations
Python async, LangChain basics, first RAG chain with Chroma + OpenAI. Complete: LangChain QuickStart tutorial.
2
Week 3-4: LangGraph Basics
Build your first graph, understand state, add a conditional edge, add a tool. Follow: LangGraph tutorials on the official site.
3
Week 5-6: RAG in LangGraph
Implement Advanced RAG with grading, hallucination checks, and fallbacks. Add checkpointing and multi-turn memory.
4
Week 7-8: Agent Patterns
Build ReAct agent, Plan-and-Execute agent. Add human-in-the-loop. Explore LangSmith for debugging.
5
Month 3: Enterprise
Multi-agent supervisor system, security guardrails, RAGAS evaluation, production deployment with PostgreSQL checkpointing.
6
Ongoing: Production
Monitor with LangSmith, A/B test retrieval strategies, collect user feedback, iterate on prompts, optimize costs.

  • LangGraph Docs — Official tutorials and API reference
  • LangSmith — Observability and evaluation platform
  • LangChain Docs — Document loaders, retrievers, chains
  • RAGAS — RAG evaluation framework
